Gnosis Pay Breach Exposes User Data, Triggering Immediate Phishing Alerts

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Gnosis Pay, a fintech project from Britain, officially confirmed that data belonging to holders of its retail Visa debit cards was compromised. The breach which took place on November 3 continues the trend of this fall, when attackers no longer need to go after blockchains or smart contracts. It’s far easier for them to hit the traditional fiat infra that links digital assets to the real world.

Institutional partners and investors can exhale as the company’s enterprise and white-label B2B platform is running normally. For ordinary users, the key point is that the crypto in their wallets is safe, as the company said funds were not affected either. Card issuer Monavate Limited, which is regulated by the UK’s Financial Conduct Authority, continues to operate as well. The hackers walked away with data only.

This time the unauthorized access involved a legacy B2C card processing system. The Gnosis Pay platform was already being prepared for retirement, with a full shutdown scheduled for December 20. According to the official explanation, after the hack happened, Gnosis Pay’s security team moved fast to contain the threat and cut off access via the compromised keys within two hours.

The Gnosis Pay incident rounds out a trilogy of major leaks this fall and exposes a weak spot shared by crypto-fiat gateways. The timeline of the past few weeks points to a clear pattern evident in Ledger, Trezor, SafePal and Keystone leaks throughout 2026.

Gnosis Pay has finished emailing affected users with a detailed list of the exposed data. Until the system goes offline next month, the company is operating in emergency mode with transaction monitoring being upped, and 3D Secure verification is now mandatory for all online payments.

Of course, the company has also issued an urgent phishing alert. Armed with purchase details, addresses and personal data, scammers are already starting to pose as official customer support. The Gnosis Pay team urges users to be suspicious of all incoming requests and reminds them that “we and our Partner Apps will never ask” for security credentials such as seed phrases, private keys, PINs or full card numbers to verify or cancel transactions. Finally, any account changes are legitimate only when users make them directly within the official app.

Ready for Moore? Mikey Moore poised to return to Cologne starting XI

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Cologne head coach René Wagner has suggested that Tottenham Hotspur loanee Mikey Moore could return to the starting lineup for Sunday’s Bundesliga encounter against Bayer Leverkusen. The 19-year-old Englishman has recently found himself relegated to the bench, but Wagner remains convinced that the youngster can make an important contribution to his team’s attacking play.

Speaking at Friday’s pre-match press conference, Wagner indicated that Moore stands a good chance of starting against the Werkself in an important league fixture that qualifies as a regional derby.

“The possibility exists, yes,” Wagner explained when asked whether Moore could return to the starting XI. “The chance that he’ll start is not small.”

The news offers encouragement for a player who has struggled to build upon his promising introduction to German football. Moore made an impressive Bundesliga debut against VfB Stuttgart in September, but has subsequently found consistent attacking contributions difficult to produce.

As examined in Get German Football News‘ assessment of Moore’s Bundesliga debut, the England youth international immediately injected creativity and attacking impetus into Cologne’s performance after entering at halftime during a 4-1 defeat in Stuttgart.

His lively showing earned him a place in Wagner’s starting lineup against Werder Bremen the following week. The Cologne coach also expressed considerable enthusiasm for the prospect of Moore developing an attacking partnership with Said El Mala.

The teenager’s subsequent development has not been entirely straightforward. After three consecutive Bundesliga starts, Moore was left on the bench for October’s 1-0 defeat against Eintracht Frankfurt as Wagner opted for a more defensive tactical approach.

The Tottenham loanee subsequently returned to the starting lineup against Schalke 04 and featured from the beginning in Köln’s emphatic 5-0 DFB-Pokal victory over Union Berlin. Nevertheless, Moore was once again relegated to the bench when Köln faced Union in the Bundesliga just five days later. Wagner introduced the Englishman in the 61st minute of the 1-1 draw, but the youngster struggled to make a decisive impact.

Wagner subsequently explained that workload management, Moore’s inconsistent performances, and tactical considerations had all influenced his decision. The teenager remains without a Bundesliga goal or assist despite showing flashes of the creativity and technical ability that prompted Köln to pursue his signature throughout the summer.

Wagner nevertheless believes that a breakthrough could arrive at any moment.

“Ultimately, in football, you need that one moment when things simply start to flow,” the Cologne coach explained.

The trainer praised Moore’s continued commitment during training and his determination to contribute when introduced from the bench. Wagner nevertheless identified positional discipline as an area requiring improvement, noting that Moore had occasionally abandoned useful attacking positions against Union’s deep defensive structure.

The Englishman must learn to remain patient and occupy spaces in which his attacking abilities can be most effective.

“He’s young and needs that one moment,” Wagner emphasized. “We believe it will come.”

Moore’s potential return comes as Cologne attempt to overcome a difficult period in front of goal. As previously reported by GGFN, the club suffered a significant double injury setback during the October international break when Thijs Dallinga and Ragnar Ache were both injured in a friendly against Viktoria Köln.

Dallinga’s serious knee injury has ruled him out for an extended period, while Ache has been working toward a return from a muscular problem. The absences have required Wagner to experiment with different attacking combinations, placing additional responsibility on Moore, Said El Mala, and the club’s other available forwards.

A starting opportunity against Leverkusen would provide Moore with another chance to rediscover the attacking confidence he demonstrated during his first Bundesliga appearances.

It appears that the young Englishman’s club is conce again “Ready for Moore”.

Italian design moguls to launch Fondazione Vezza public art project in Piedmont next September

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A new contemporary art hub nestled among the ruins of a medieval monastery is due to open in northern Italy next September. The Fondazione Vezza project, founded by the Piedmontese entrepreneur Sandra Vezza and her son Charley, will be based in Monforte d’Alba in the Langhe wine region, around 60 kilometres southeast of Turin.

The pair plan to develop a trail over the next decade through the woodland on Colle San Pietro hill; two site-specific large-scale works by the US artist Kathleen Ryan and the Italian sculptor Alessandro Pessoli will inaugurate the project next autumn. The trail will include the ruins of the monastery of San Pietro, an early medieval complex first documented in 1014.

Flavio del Monte, formerly director of institutional relations at Massimo de Carlo gallery in Milan, will oversee the Fondazione Vezza project. “Each year, one or more artists will be invited to engage with the site, its woodland, its paths, existing architecture and history. Over approximately ten years, the works will shape a route that will continue to grow and change without losing the site’s original character,” says a project statement.

Sandra and Charley Vezza will fund the production of the works; budget costs remain confidential. Asked if the foundation will own the pieces produced, a spokesperson said: “Yes, they will be installed permanently onsite. The target audience is really everyone, the widest possible audience.” The site will be open to the public and free to enter.

“With its opening in 2027, the Foundation will begin a long-term exploration of the relationship between contemporary art, the landscape, and the Vezza family’s vision, shaped by the artists invited to contribute to the site over time,” adds the statement. Project supporters include the Comune di Monforte, the Comune di Milano and Gagosian Gallery.

Sandra and Charley Vezza founded the Italian Radical Design Group in 2022, a Barolo-based company established with “the specific aim of preserving and promoting historic Italian design brands”, says the company website. The same year the group acquired Memphis Milano, the 1980s post-modern design collective. In 2012, the Vezzas bought Gufram, an Italian radical design brand founded in the 1960s, known for its pop art-inspired furniture.

#AskNewser: CNN, NBC News and NewsNation Executives Reflect on Their Election Night Coverage

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

As the dust settles from a historic midterm election that saw the Democratic Party surf a blue wave to capture both the Senate and the House, news outlets are downshifting from special coverage and settling back into regular programming.

With 31 million viewers tuning in Tuesday night across 13 networks, TVNewser recently spoke to NBC News’ evp, creative, innovation & field production Marc Greenstein, whose network was the No.1 network during primetime in total viewers and the Adults 25-54 demo, as well as CNN’s svp, Washington & special events programming Abigail Crutchfield, whose network was the most-watched cable news network in the prized demo, and NewsNation’s political director Chris Stirewalt, whose network in coordination with Decision Desk HQ was the first one to call both the House and the Senate for the Democrats, for a post-election AskNewser on what worked well during their live election coverage.

TVNewser: What worked well from a coverage and technology standpoint on election night?

Greenstein: Steve Kornacki continues to be the best at breaking down the vote, providing context and helping viewers understand not just what is happening, but where the night is headed.

We combined editorial and technology in a new way, allowing Steve to contribute to the linear broadcast while simultaneously hosting his own dedicated streaming feed from the same studio as Savannah Guthrie and Tom Llamas. This allowed us to produce two distinct broadcasts just feet apart, each tailored to its audience, without losing the editorial connection between our talent.

Extensive planning for multiple election scenarios allowed us to react quickly as race calls came in. We deployed multiple correspondents across nearly every battleground state, covering both voting developments and candidate headquarters. This gave us a strong presence in the places driving the story and allowed us to bring firsthand reporting and reaction into the broadcast throughout the night.

We played more than 35,000 graphics across our zones and platforms, delivering results and analysis with speed and clarity. We brought together broadcast, streaming and digital coverage under a consistent editorial and visual approach, while tailoring the experience to each platform. The launch of our dedicated vertical video feed was another important step in reaching audiences in new ways without compromising the depth or quality of our journalism.

Cruthchfield: It was having all of our experts and talent together in one place. It gave the DC bureau great energy, and it was fun to watch the reporters and anchors work together to unpack the results.

Our director, Reza Baktar, designed a phenomenal set that gave us more mobility than ever and made it easy to get to any data set we needed at any moment. I also loved giving John King the ability to analyze the data at the magic wall or to bring his mini magic wall over to our analyst tables to be a part of the conversation. And I loved King’s new vertical magic wall, which opened up new storytelling options.

Stirewalt: You can’t beat the cards. We had such an advantage by having Decision Desk HQ (DDHQ) on our side. I think we did a good job of bringing viewers into the process and explaining what we were doing, but when you get down to it, the advantage of being so far ahead of the competition was just amazing.

TN: What was the biggest surprise of the night?

Greenstein: The biggest surprise was how quickly we were able to call control of the Senate. While there was a general sense that it could be a good night for Democrats, I think the early call caught many of us a little off guard. That said, we had planned for a wide range of scenarios and were prepared to stay on air throughout the night across the broadcast network, NBC News Now and Kornacki Cam.

Another pleasant surprise was how well the AI audio technology worked, allowing us to produce the linear and streaming broadcasts simultaneously from the same studio. We had limited time to rehearse with the technology, so there were certainly some nerves heading into the night. But it performed remarkably well and allowed us to pull off something we had never done before.

Cruthchfield: I think perhaps how quickly we were able to project the major results of the night. We always plan for every scenario, but this Tuesday we got answers from the voters quickly.

Stirewalt: How much work I wasted preparing for close elections in so many places. I was buckled in for tight contests in battleground states. We found out by 8:30 p.m. ET or so that what we really needed to get good at was going deep on places like Mississippi.

TN: Share the tricks you used to keep yourself awake and alert on Tuesday night.

Greenstein: Coffee, Diet Coke, and the adrenaline of being in the director’s chair! In my role, I rarely get the opportunity to direct. The last time was Election Night 2024. So getting back in the chair and working alongside the most talented people in the business, both on air and behind the scenes, pushes me to be at the top of my game. And I love every minute of it.

Cruthchfield: A lot of hot tea, a phenomenal team, and a big bag of gummy bears.

Stirewalt: I don’t need any outside stimulants. When the results started pouring in, I was so thrilled at how DDHQ and our team were doing that I was just high on life.

Mark Mwachiro is a TVNewser contributor who has been writing for the blog since March 2022. Based out of New York, Mark has also contributed to ADWEEK.

Opendoor Says It ‘Rebuilt’ System With AI, Triples Home Purchases

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

After over a year of working as CEO, Opendoor’s Kaz Nejatian reported significant growth in revenue and market share during the iBuyer’s Q3 earnings call, and attributed the gains to a shift into AI infrastructure as the company says it has found ways to flip more homes without overpaying.

“This past quarter we rebuilt this entire system at Opendoor. We built an AI system that determines a specific repair plan for every single home before we buy it,” he stated. “Until August, our resale process was similar to literally everyone else in real estate. Pricing every home we owned basically lived in glorified spreadsheets with over 70 columns.”

As real estate incumbents have largely moved on from the iBuying craze that swept over the pandemic boom market, Opendoor has continued to push forward with a more nuanced approach to the model, offering a wider array of services to home sellers and partnerships with agents.

While acknowledging the company still has a long way to go, Nejatian is claiming the turnaround is working, even amid what he characterized as the worst years for housing “in a long time.”

“We’re winning the market without having to make riskier offers,” he said.

A lot of that comes from technology, along with a stronger focus on moving homes faster with less investment. Nejatian pointed out the differences between scaling with employees than with technology. He stated that increasing scale with the traditional method of buying homes involved hiring more employees, and a longer process.

“Rather than having people spending their days looking at pictures and doing mundane tasks, our people now each manage an army of agents,” he added. “In July, we had 5,400 homes. That’s 5,400 sellers sitting in a queue waiting for days for a human to confirm the price on their home. Two weeks ago, that queue was zero, and it will remain zero.”

He also homed in on the iBuyer’s new initiative—Buy with Opendoor—which launched in beta earlier this week. Although the Q3 earnings predates the program’s release, Opendoor CFO Christy Schwartz believes the initiative can open the company up to the broader market.

“Our emerging Buy with Opendoor offering can help buyers whether or not they’re buying a home from Opendoor,” she noted. “We see an opportunity to extend title and escrow to customers who aren’t buying or selling through Opendoor. Similar to listings, the offerings extend our reach beyond our home acquisition business and create opportunities to earn without taking ownership of a home.”

Previously, Nejatian came into the role with an aggressive turnaround mindset, touting an “Opendoor 2.0” initiative to bring the company back to significant profitability. The third quarter earnings may reflect the fruits of his labor, as homes purchased more than quadrupled.

“When a market slows down, companies are left with a cost structure and a system built for a different business. That’s the disease that Opendoor 2.0 is designed to cure,” he added. “That’s why I spend so much of my time on these calls talking about operator efficiency. When we say we’re investing in growth, we are building the capacity to handle the growth that will arrive.”

Overall, Opendoor’s third quarter saw a revenue of $1.02 billion, up 12% from last year’s revenue of $915 million and up 16% from the previous quarter of $883 million.

The number of homes purchased skyrocketed as well, increasing by 316% year-over-year from 1,169 in 2025 to 4,865 in 2026.

At the same time, though, Nejatian acknowledged the company is “behind schedule” on reaching profitability by the end of the year, blaming the slow market.

Opendoor’s stock fell sharply after the report, down about 17% at press time.

Again, Nejatian posited that the increases in efficiency with AI infrastructure have contributed to Q3’s results.

“Not that long ago, around 80% of the assessments we did on homes required an Opendoor employee, which could take many weeks. Today, that 80% is 2%,” he said. “In Q3, we purchased more than four times as many homes as a year ago, and our operation expense was basically the same.”

Operation expenses per acquired home fell to $4,000, down 36% from last year’s expenses of $6,300. The marketing expenses per acquired home dropped at a higher rate, down 57% to $2,800, compared to 2025’s $6,500.

Mortgage originations—or the process in which a borrower goes through to receive a home loan—increased over 400% from quarter-to-quarter, although the Q3 earnings may not reflect the full weight of the Federal Reserve’s rate hike in mid-September. While the latest data does show mortgage rates inching down slowly each week, it’s unclear as to when rates will go below 7%.

“Historically, most of our economics have come from buying and reselling homes complimented by title and escrow,” Schwartz described. “Our emerging mortgage offerings allow us to earn more from each relationship while meeting a critical customer need and making the transaction easier.”

Opendoor’s EBITDA loss also improved slightly on a yearly basis. Q3 2026 had an EBITDA loss of $31 million, while last year had an EBITDA of $33 million. The adjusted EBITDA margin also decreased, now at 3% compared to last year’s margin of 3.6%. While better than last year, Schwartz acknowledged that the latest data is still far off from last quarter’s EBITDA loss of $4 million.

“Most of the sequential decline came from the homes we bought in the spring selling into a much weaker market along with deeper price cuts as we chose to clear rather than hold,” she said.

Looking to the future, Schwartz expects margins to decline again in the fourth quarter, and states that they will continue to sell through the remaining spring acquisitions into Q1 2027.

She also expects revenue to grow by at least 40% on a yearly basis in the next quarter. Although revenue may increase, Schwartz noted that marketing does contain a seasonal aspect, and operating expenses will likely increase due to investments and growth.

“Comparing year-over-year, roughly a third of the increase in our expected loss is the additional marketing, and the remaining two-thirds is primarily higher holding costs as we’ve been rebuilding our inventory,” she concluded.

“Our goal is to be adjusted net income positive on a 12-month-forward basis at the end of this year,” Nejatian continued. “We’re a bit behind on this today, but we’re going to go through some walls and catch up.”

Prior to joining RISMedia, Desirae wrote stories geared toward policy issues for the Connecticut Mirror. She also worked as a staff writer for The Daily Campus, UConn's student-run newspaper.

PSA: Want To Learn Link's Ocarina Tunes? Nintendo's Got The Sheet Music For All Of Them

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Whether you have been playing Ocarina of Time on Switch 2, watching the trailers, or just been in the ether of its release, you almost certainly have an ocarina melody or two stuck in your head right now. For those who want to channel their earworm into a lesson, Nintendo has shared the sheet music for each of Link's in-game tunes, so you can stop whistling Zelda's Lullaby and learn to play it instead.

Nintendo previously announced that it'd share music how-tos via the Zelda Notes app, so you could work out how to play the tunes on any real-world ocarinas you may have accumulated over the years. But look, all of them are also available on the game's Japanese website, so you don't need to worry about unlocking them in-game before you start learning!

Alongside the sheet music, the website also includes specific ocarina finger placement to follow along with on the titular instrument.

Oh yes, and just in case these 12 tunes aren't already ingrained in your heads, the site (and app) also features a handy 'Play' button, so you can listen to how each of them is meant to sound before taking a stab at 'em yourself.

Of course, you might be waiting to learn any of these until you have one of the official tie-in ocarinas in your hands. Pre-orders for HORI's electric model went live back in September, and we are now seeing orders slowly trickle out for the playable version in select regional Nintendo stores.

Hidden in the Viral Last Ditch Debacle: How to Keep Lesbian Bars Alive

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

By September of this year, Jackie Matellian had spent months living in her truck while working several jobs to keep Last Ditch, a lesbian bar in Greenfield, Massachusetts, open. She was the last remaining original founder, and the bar was in deep financial trouble. According to reporting by The Boston Globe, workers had gone unpaid, Matellian was carrying a $50,000 start-up loan, and the landlord wanted $3,900 in overdue rent.

Last Ditch had been built to do things most bars don’t even attempt. It welcomed lesbians, transsexuals, and other queer people who couldn’t afford the cover, offered a free store with food, clothing and harm-reduction supplies, and required KN95 masks or better. For immunocompromised patrons, those precautions made it possible to have a night out without gambling with their health. The bar’s radical inclusiveness was part of why people cared about it—and part of what its owners were struggling to finance it.

Desperate for cash, the bar’s management announced a policy shift with a cheery Instagram post: Saturday nights would become mask-optional. The gamble backfired. By treating accessibility as negotiable, the bar alienated immunocompromised regulars without attracting enough new customers to pay the rent. Follow-up stumbles culminated in a marathon three-and-a-half-hour community meeting that collapsed without a resolution. Rather than saving the venue, the policy pivot left Last Ditch with the worst of both worlds: fractured community trust and an unresolved financial crisis.

The drama became national entertainment, right up until October 26, when Last Ditch announced its soft closure.

The spectators moved on, but one issue remains: expecting one struggling lesbian bar to compensate for the failures of an entire social infrastructure is absurd. The irony is that Western Massachusetts built the blueprint for combating exactly this.

Women in the area have been finding ways to share the costs of lesbian social life for decades. UMass Amherst documents more than 400 lesbian and feminist groups active in the region between 1968 and 1978. Northampton had the Common Womon Club, Lesbian Gardens, Womonfyre Books, and the Nutcracker’s Suite self-defense dojo. These were places women built to make their social lives and organizing possible outside institutions that routinely excluded them.

In 1976, the women behind Common Womon pooled money to buy property through Ceres Inc., the nonprofit they’d formed, then financed renovations with help from donations and a loan. The adjacent storefront provided rental income and housed the Nutcracker’s Suite, then Womonfyre Books, so control of the property also gave the women a way to make room for other projects and help pay for the place they shared. Common Womon also collected sliding-scale membership dues, while the Valley Women’s History Collaborative lists benefits for the Common Womon Club that helped keep it going.

The womon-spelling and the names are fun to inherit; I also want us to inherit the ability to put actual premises under our control. For Last Ditch, that begins with finding out whether a new set of owners can remain in the building on terms the business can afford.

Matellian had already told the Globe she planned to turn the bar into a cooperative. For that to relieve the pressure on her, new members would need to take responsibility for work she’d been doing and agree on how the existing debt would be handled. But calling it a cooperative wouldn’t do much for Matellian if she was still the person everyone called whenever something went wrong. Sharing authority has to include sharing responsibility for the work, with limits on what any one person is expected to carry.

Sharing ownership would give people more power over decisions and occasions to disagree about them. Common Womon closed in 1982, while Womonfyre lasted until 1989. Womonfyre faced harassment and threats from outside the lesbian community as well as conflicts over sexual politics and what the shop should sell. Its archive preserves those disputes alongside bank statements and instructions for running the store.

A cooperative at Last Ditch would need procedures for making decisions in tandem with conflict resolution. Which is why I like that the Womonfyre archive makes room for instructions on running the shop alongside political disputes. A disagreement over how to run a bar doesn’t become abuse because people are arguing. Treating every conflict as a verdict on someone’s character makes it harder to agree on a change, because the argument can continue long after the conduct has stopped or the policy has been reversed.

Making that possible at Last Ditch requires paid training and a written account of all this work. A cooperative whose members can debate the mission for four hours but can’t cover a shift or pay its debt will end up crushing the same woman behind the bar.

If Last Ditch is to survive, its next incarnation must trade endless character trials for operational infrastructure: shared labor, transparent books, and creative financing strategies.

If one thing the Globe’s reporting revealed, it is that one commercial bar bearing the weight of an entire community’s social safety crushes everyone under impossible expectations. Instead, we must look for ways to anchor the politics in practical economics, share the burden, and build a room strong enough to hold the argument without tearing down the house.

Anyone asking the community to save Last Ditch needs to be prepared to share control of what survives.

Opendoor says it’s behind schedule but plans to catch up by 2027

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Opendoor reported a $66 million adjusted net loss and a $218 million net loss in Q3 2026, despite 12% revenue growth to $1.022 billion. The company said it is behind schedule on its profitability goal and is leaning on capital-light products, AI pricing and mortgage growth.  AI Summary

Opendoor is behind schedule. Since taking over the company a little over a year ago, CEO Kaz Nejatian has promised that the firm would achieve adjusted net income positive status on a 12-month go-forward basis by the end of 2026.

But the recent housing market downturn, triggered by the rapid rise in mortgage rates, has proven to be a bit of a roadblock. “We are currently behind but we intend to catch up,” the iBuyer’s third quarter 2026 earnings report explained.

During the quarter, Opendoor recorded an adjusted net loss of $66 million and an actual net loss of $218 million, up from a $90 million net loss a year ago. This comes even as the company reported a 12% annual increase in revenue to $1.022 billion.

“A year ago, I told you we would build a different Opendoor — faster, stronger, fully committed to our mission of tilting the world in favor of homeowners,” Nejatian said during his firm’s earnings call with investors and analysts on Thursday.

“We have spent the past year building a machine, and that machine is getting better every single day. We are not done yet. It is not perfect, but it is getting better every single day. The question is this: Is Opendoor in a better economic condition than it was a year ago? The answer is obviously yes.”

Nejatian’s confidence comes from his belief that Opendoor has become more resilient over the past year. This includes ways in which the company has worked to serve more sellers without adding more homes to the company’s balance sheet.

“This past week, around half of the first offers our direct-to-consumer sellers accepted were listing or Cash Now, More Later. That lets us serve more sellers with less of our capital exposed to changes in the market,” Nejatian said.

In the past 12 months, the company has done over $1 billion in Cash Now, More Later volume.

“We have built more ways to meet sellers’ needs and have made it easier for them to understand their options and make the choice they want for their own lives,” Nejatian said. “Sellers can now adjust the offer slides and make the choice that is good for their families.”

Nejatian noted that every other time Opendoor has tried to scale, it has done so by purchasing more properties, but that isn’t the case this time.

“We proved that we can grow without buying growth. That’s the single most important thing that has happened over the last year. We’re changing that with our capital-light products,” Nejatian said.

Still, Opendoor did purchase far more homes in Q3 2026 than it did in Q3 2025, with a 316% year-over-year increase to 4,865 homes. The company’s signed acquisition volume represented an estimated 0.56% of market share in the addressable U.S. market in Q3 2026 — more than four times higher than a year ago.

On the sales side, Opendoor sold 2,728 homes, an increase of 160 homes compared to a year ago. At the end of the quarter, the iBuyer had 7,596 homes in its inventory, up from 3,139 homes a year ago. While the increase in inventory could be seen as cause for concern, especially heading into the slower winter housing market, Opendoor noted that homes listed for sale for 120 days represented just 18% of inventory at the end of the quarter, down from 50% a year ago.

Despite the progress, Nejatian said the company still has work left to do on unit economics and resale velocity, something it needs to focus on as it strives for more resiliency.

“We’re not as good as we want to be, but we’re in a much better place,” he said. “When the market moves against you and you own homes, there’s two things you can do. You can sell fast, or you can hold and wait and pray for the market to recover. We choose to clear.

“The decline in late August and September knocked us off track on execution a little bit because we turned our attention to clearance rather than margin improvement. It costs something real in a quarter like this, but that’s the right way to run this company for the long run.”

To help with some of these challenges, Nejatian said Opendoor has turned to AI to help improve speed while still scaling. Some of these tools include an AI model that determines repair plans for every home to hit the market. The company said the system has been outperforming the old process of following a standard repair manual.

Opendoor said it also built a system to help price homes. While humans still have final judgment, the software does the work of pulling applicable, comparable homes.

Nejatian also said that Opendoor is focusing on its mortgage division. In Q3, Opendoor said originations from its in-house lender were up 400% year over year. This comes as the iBuyer’s lender is now licensed in 25 states, compared to none a year ago.

In addition, the company also launched a beta test of Buy with Opendoor in the San Francisco Bay Area this week. The product allows the company to earn mortgage revenue on properties not affiliated with Opendoor in anyway.

Looking to the future, Nejatian said Opendoor is hoping to build out title and escrow services — not only to add more efficiencies to Opendoor’s in-house transactions but to add additional revenue streams by serving consumers not in its ecosystem.

“We need to grow, use less capital per transaction, and sell homes faster. Those are things we can control, and that is what we are focused on. What matters to us is solving the core problems of our business, and those are what we need to focus on,” Nejatian said.

Anthropic IPO Valuation Expectations Fall After Technology Selloff

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

CNBC reported on November 6, 2026 that some Anthropic investors were lowering their valuation expectations after the technology selloff, with one putting a more likely range at $1 trillion to $1.25 trillion. Anthropic was still expected to begin its IPO roadshow in the week of November 9, 2026.

That range is below both the $1.5 trillion valuation one investor called a “stretch” and Anthropic’s recent $2 trillion objective. The estimate could change with market conditions. The investors requested anonymity because they were not authorized to discuss the matter, and Anthropic declined to comment. Preparations are underway for a market debut expected within weeks.

The estimates add detail to the investor push for better Anthropic IPO terms that Reuters reported on November 5, 2026. That earlier account described demands for a more attractive valuation and uncertainty over whether the roadshow timetable could still support a November 2026 listing.

Two pressures frame the shift: concern about the scale of AI companies’ spending and the broad market reversal after the midterm elections. On November 5, 2026, the Nasdaq lost more than 5%, its largest one-session decline in 19 months, amid fears that Democrats’ increased power in Washington and public opposition to AI could slow data-center construction. Selling pressure had begun before the election, with chipmakers and neocloud companies leading the Nasdaq to its worst October since March 2025.

D.A. Davidson analyst Gil Luria told CNBC that investors would expect the IPO to price at least at the low end of previous expectations. “The best way to set Anthropic up for success is to price the deal well,” he said, adding that the market needs the IPO to go well and that the bankers on the deal understand that.

Anthropic announced on October 26, 2026 that it had filed the registration statement for its proposed IPO of Class A common stock. Its amended preliminary prospectus, filed on November 2, 2026, records an application to list the shares on the Nasdaq Global Select Market. The amendment is preliminary, with the share count and price range undetermined; the securities cannot be sold until the registration statement becomes effective.

The prospectus reports $17.0 billion of revenue for the three months ended September 30, 2026, and year-over-year revenue growth of 1,127%. Compute and infrastructure expenses grew 382% during the same period.

As of and subsequent to September 30, 2026, Anthropic had entered into purchase commitments and lease agreements totaling approximately $895.4 billion, substantially all related to compute and infrastructure. Approximately $69.8 billion is due in the twelve months after that date.

As of September 30, 2026, Anthropic had raised an aggregate $141.1 billion of capital and held $95.6 billion in cash and cash equivalents and $36.3 billion in short-term investments. A May 28, 2026 Series H announcement provides a private-market reference point: Anthropic raised $65 billion at a $965 billion post-money valuation, in a round led by Altimeter Capital, Dragoneer, Greenoaks, and Sequoia Capital.

The lower valuation range and the filing’s figures now define the roadshow’s pricing debate. The central question is how public investors balance Anthropic’s rapid revenue growth and existing capital resources against the scale of its future spending when pricing the offering.

American Airlines Vs. United First Class: Boring Chicken Dinners That I’d Happily Order Again

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

I realize if you just read my blog it looks like I’ll eat anything thrown in front of me and to some extent that is true on airplane, but a pair of flights, one on American Airlines, the other on United Airlines, featured chicken dinners much closer to my what I usually eat at home. Let’s compare them in this week’s Meal of the Week.

Let’s start with American Airlines. I was flying from Los Angeles to Chicago and received a same-day upgrade (as a mere Advantage Gold!) and thus was not able to pre-ordered. Onboard the choices were chicken or pasta (described as a chicken breast with rice or pasta shells with tomato sauce). I chose chicken.

The chicken breast was covered in a white sauce and served with white rice on the side and green beans on top. On the side, a small green salad with sun-dried tomatoes and a ramekin with what appeared to be parsnips, carrots, and cabbage with croutons…at least I think so (whatever it was, it was nice).

While not an exciting dinner, I really appreciated just eating a lean chicken breast and green beans…it’s very similar to what we eat at least a couple nights per week at home.

Dessert was raspberry sorbet…I miss the Häagen-Dazs ice cream that used to be on these flights (and the real ice cream sundaes before that).

On to United. Same route. The current “signature entree” out of Los Angeles is described as:

Pollo asado chicken thigh Pollo asado chicken thigh with charred corn farro, street corn style zucchini and asado sauce

The AA dish was fine, but this folks was excellent. The flavor of the chicken was excellent and the corn farro and zucchini were really nice…we grow zucchini in our garden at home and cook it in the oven with our chicken so this really felt close to home. The corn was a nice treat and I ate every bit of it.

The sale on the side had sun-dried tomatoes and sliced cucumbers and the meal also came with a cornmeal roll and a slice of “hot chocolate” cheesecake, which I passed on.

Both were rather boring meals, but both were very delicious. Simple is often better.

NASA's Twin Spacecraft Will Execute Powered Slingshot Maneuvers on Their Way to Mars

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A pair of NASA are spacecraft are about to perform a slingshot maneuver above Earth, and some of us might get to see them. They're on their way to Mars, where they'll dig more deeply into one of the Solar System's puzzles: how Mars lost its ancient atmosphere. The pair are unique, because once they reach the planet, they'll be the first spacecraft to "fly" in formation around Mars.

It's becoming clear, if not certain, that Mars was once warm and wet. It may have been habitable, even inhabited by simple life. But something happened and the planet is now cold and dead.

To be warm and wet, it must have had a much thicker atmosphere than the thin, CO2 atmosphere it has now. Scientists who study the iron-red world postulate that UV radiation from the Sun drove Mars' ancient atmosphere into space. That only happened after Mars' molten core cooled, and the planet lost its protective magnetic field. Once exposed to the full force of the Sun's UV radiation, the atmosphere was doomed.

Though its global magnetosphere is long gone, Mars still has patches of magnetic fields called crustal magnetic fields, or remnant magnetic fields. They aren't created by a dynamo; they're local magnetic fields generated by iron-rich chunks of the planet's crust. When Mars' dynamo failed billions of years ago, these chunks locked-in the magnetic orientation of the ancient crust. These surface fields on Mars also generate similar patches of magnetic fields in the planet's upper atmosphere. All these fields aren't anywere near as strong as the ancient dynamo, but they're enough to work with.

This map of Mars' crustal magnetism was created with data from NASA's now-defunct Mars Global Surveyor. In simple terms, it's a colour-coded map of the magnetic strength. Blue is weak, red is strong. Image Credit: By NASA - Public Domain.

While the broad picture of Mars' atmosphere loss is clear, the gritty details are not. The NASA spacecraft on their way to orbit Mars are called Escape and Plasma Acceleration and Dynamics Explorers (ESCAPADE). They're twins, named Red and Blue, and they'll measure how the solar wind interacts with the remnants of Mars' ancient magnetic field.

Soon the spacecraft will approach Earth. At their closest, they'll be between about 965 to 1,770 kilometers above the surface, and they'll use their thrusters to boost their speed. Those boosts will combined with Earth's gravity, and they'll slingshot their way to Mars. The first will do so on November 7th, the second on November 9th. These trans-Mars injection burns will last about 9 minutes and will increase their speed from about 10.14 km/s to 10.78 km/s. That's an increase of more than 2,000 km/hour.

These maneuvers are necessary because ESCAPADE didn't launch in the usual Mars launch window. Those windows occur about every 26 months. So the spacecraft had to loiter near the Earth-Sun Lagrange point 2 for a while, then approach Earth and use these burns to power their way to Mars. This led to some bonus science, where the spacecraft flew through and observed Earth's magnetotail. They were the first spacecraft to do so, and those observations will be shared soon.

A natural question arises about ESCAPADE: Why are there two spacecraft?

Solar wind interactions with magnetic fields can change on very short timescales. A single spacecraft wouldn't be able to tell if changes it observes are due to changes in these interactions, or changes in its position. A pair of spacecraft flying in formation removes this uncertainty. NASA calls this formation the "String of Pearls."

This animation shows the ESCAPADE spacecraft in their String of Pearls formation above Mars.

The spacecraft will fly the same orbit at varying distances from each other. But that's just for the first part of the mission, Science Phase A, which will last about 6 months. In Science Phase B, ESCAPADE's Blue and Red units will work more independently. Blue will lower its orbit to about 7,000 km and Red will raise its orbit to about 10,000 km. That will last about 5 months, until the end of ESCAPADE's nominal science mission. The spacecraft should remain in operation for many years after that, and NASA will have some decisions to make then.

The spacecraft will be visible to some observers on the ground, though a telescope will almost certainly be needed. They'll present as a pair of dim, refrigerator-sized objects moving against the starry background. Not super-exciting, but interesting nonetheless.

It's eerie to think about a planet like Mars losing its habitability and dying. It tells us something about the search for life, and about Earth. There may be multitudes of planets that were habitable for short periods of timeâmaybe hosting simple lifeâthat were extinguished before any complexity could arise. It puts life on Earth in perspective for its rarity. Earth has been habitable for billions of years, and, against probabilities, complex life has fluorished for hundreds of millions of years, even longer depending on which definition is used.

Having one of these extinguished planets next door is a rare opportunity to understand how this happens, and ESCAPADE will take full advantage of that opportunity.

Evan Gough is a science-loving guy with no formal education who loves Earth, forests, hiking, and heavy music. He's guided by Carl Sagan's quote: "Understanding is a kind of ecstasy."

The Best Gifts for Minimalists Who Appreciate Good Design

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Shopping for someone who prefers fewer things can be tricky. After all, the last thing a minimalist wants is another object taking up space without serving a purpose. The best gifts for minimalists aren’t necessarily the smallest or simplest, though. They’re the ones that make everyday life a little better, whether through thoughtful functionality, beautiful materials, or a detail that makes an ordinary object feel extraordinary.

With that in mind, we’ve gathered gifts that offer more than good looks. Think everyday essentials with clever design features, well-made objects that age gracefully, and pieces that balance practicality with a strong point of view. Because when you’re intentional about what you own, every addition should be worth making room for.

With its rounded silhouette, matte finish, and oversized handle, Fellow’s Clyde Electric Kettle brings a sculptural quality to an everyday kitchen essential. The 1.5-liter capacity makes it practical for everything from morning tea to French-press coffee, while a simple LED switch keeps operation refreshingly straightforward. It’s a countertop appliance that doesn’t need to be tucked away between uses.

The Clyde Electric Kettle is available at Fellow.com for $99.95.

A tiny bird emerges to announce the hour and half hour, while a built-in light sensor keeps things quiet when the room gets dark. Designed to sit on a shelf or hang on a wall, it’s a charming combination of Japanese simplicity and old-fashioned whimsy.

The Cuckoo Clock is available at muji.us for $79.90.

With its weathered texture and classical silhouette, Joon Loloi’s Sumaira Bowl looks like an artifact unearthed from another era. But its construction is decidedly contemporary. Handmade in Jaipur, India, from EcoMix, a material incorporating recycled paper, the sculptural vessel brings depth and character to minimalist interiors without relying on color or ornament. The Vati Vase is available at the-citizenry.com for $79 (wide) or $99 (tall).

A pair of softly rounded ceramic platters brings quiet sophistication to the table without unnecessary embellishment. Handcrafted from stoneware, each features a satin exterior, glossy interior, and delicate hand-painted rim. The two oblong sizes are equally suited to serving appetizers, pastries, or a family-style meal, while their stackable design makes storage refreshingly simple.

The Serving Platters are available at fromourplace.com for $37.99.

For the minimalist who prefers to travel light, Bellroy’s Mag Wallet keeps the essentials close without adding unnecessary bulk. Designed to attach magnetically to MagSafe-compatible phones, the slim cardholder eliminates the need for a separate wallet while maintaining a clean, understated silhouette. Available in premium leather or a leather alternative, it’s a thoughtful combination of form and function for anyone who believes less is more.

How to Polish Wood Furniture

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 6, 2026 4:32 PM EST

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn More ›

Tips, tricks & ideas for a better home and yard, delivered to your inbox daily.

If you want to maintain the beautiful, natural look of your wood furniture, take the time to polish it. A good polishing restores the wood’s appearance, bringing out the beauty of the wood grain while helping to conceal minor scratches. Wood polish can also create a protective barrier that repels dust while shielding the finish and wood from spills. Of course, polishing only protects and prolongs the life of your wood furniture if you use the right type of polish and application technique. Use the wrong polish or apply it incorrectly and you can end up with unsightly buildup, streaks, and even permanent damage. We sought expert guidance from Saadet Turlu, founder of Wow by AlpC, a Sheridan, Wyoming-based company that specializes in high-end solid wood furniture.

There are several options when it comes to choosing a polish—or you can make your own furniture polish. Though liquid varieties are easy to apply, wax-based products offer more staying power. And while there are three main types of polish, these categories often overlap as an emulsion can contain oil, silicone, or wax:

Bobvila.com may earn a commission from purchases made through these links.

While the right polish is key to getting good results when polishing wood furniture, so too is using the right technique.

Remove all objects from the furniture, including lamps, objects and any other decor, so you can apply the polish evenly over the entire piece. You also don’t want these objects to end up with polish residue on them.

Wipe the area down with a dry microfiber cloth or tack cloth to remove any loose dust. Use a Q-tip to clean dust out of crevices around trim and furniture legs. If there is grime or oily fingerprints, use a barely damp cloth to clean thoroughly. If the piece is especially dirty, you may need to conduct a more thorough cleaning before proceeding.

Not all polish is safe for all wood finishes, and you can’t always trust the list of compatible finishes listed on the bottle. Before diving in, test the polish on an inconspicuous spot to make sure it reacts well with the wood finish. An underside area of the furniture or a corner works well.

Never spray wood polish directly onto the wood. Doing so can create streaks or puddling on the furniture surface while also creating overspray that can land on the floor or surrounding furnishings. Instead, apply a small amount of polish to a soft, lint-free microfiber towel. “Apply the polish very sparingly,” Turlu says. “Always wipe in the direction of the wood grain to ensure the product penetrates the pores evenly.” Apply sparingly. Too much polish can leave a sticky film on the wood surface.

Buffing the piece is key to getting an even, attractive finish. Switch to a separate clean dry microfiber cloth and rub the surface of the wood in the direction of the grain using light pressure. “Apply light, consistent pressure and use sweeping motions, following the direction of the wood grain,” Turlu says. “If streaks appear on the surface, it means you are either applying too much pressure or you used an excessive amount of product initially.”

While furniture polish will help to enhance the shine on your furniture and hide minor scratches, it won’t do anything for stains. To remove water stains from wood, start by identifying the type of stain your dealing with:

White water rings: Water rings are often a result of damage to the finish rather than the wood itself. The best way to deal with rings is to use a commercial water marker removes. Make sure the remover is compatible with the type of finish on the wood. If you’re not sure, test the product on a hidden part of the furniture first.

Dark water stains: Dark stains are usually an indication that the stain has reached into the wood fibers. Removing these stains requires a more extensive repair that’s better left to the pros, especially if the furniture is an antique or a family heirloom.

Aim for a polishing schedule of one to three months depending on how heavily the piece of furniture is used. You might polish a dining table or desk once a month, while you may wait three months for less heavily used pieces, like a china cabinet.

Turlu advises specifically against any products containing silicone. “While silicone may provide an immediate, artificial sheen, it penetrates permanently into the wood fibers,” he says. “If that piece of furniture ever needs to be professionally refinished, the silicone will cause the new finish to separate and fail (a nightmare for refinishers).”

A good polishing wax is almost always a better choice than a polishing oil for most furniture. Wax-based polishes create a barrier that not only enhances the look of the wood but also creates a protective barrier against water and scratches that lasts for months. A polishing oil might be easier to apply, but it remains on the surface of the wood and can attract airborne dust.

While this well-known household cleaner is generally safe to use on wood furniture, it is not a polish. It can be used to clean your wood furniture, if it is particularly grimy, before you polish it.

How Warrior 1 Can Help Relieve Your Tight Hips and Lower Back

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

(Photo: Andrew Clark; Design in Canva by Laura Harold)

In Yoga Journal’s Archives series, we share a curated collection of articles originally published in past issues beginning in 1975. This article about how to do Warrior 1 first appeared in the June 2007 issue of Yoga Journal. Find more of our Archives here.

When you have only a short time for your daily practice, which poses do you choose? Whether you opt for a simple Adho Mukha Svanasana (Downward-Facing Dog), the paired inversions Sirsasana (Headstand) and Sarvangasana (Shoulderstand), or a restorative pose to counter your hectic day, consider including another pose consistently: Virabhadrasana I (Warrior I).

I often sing the praises of this challenging pose to skeptical students who feel that it’s a lot of work without a lot of noticeable benefit, so let me explain my enthusiasm for it. In my work as a physical therapist and yoga teacher, I’ve noticed that many people in our sedentary society have tight hip flexors, which they don’t stretch much, if at all. I’m convinced that short, tight hip flexors are a major factor in many cases of chronic or recurring back pain that so many people experience in the West.

The hip flexors become tight when the hips are constantly placed in a flexed position—think sitting, driving, or sleeping in the fetal position. Like any other muscles, hip flexors will shorten structurally if they are not stretched. However, if you incorporate Virabhadrasana I into your regular practice, you’ll stretch the hip flexors, helping them gradually return to their normal length and easing a source of backaches.

The hip flexor muscles include the iliacus, psoas, rectus femoris, tensor fasciae latae, sartorius, and, in some situations, the short adductors pectines and adductor brevis. All of these muscles (except the adductors mentioned) cross the front of the hip and are active in hip flexion, which pulls the femur (thighbone) and torso closer together. The hip flexes when you perform a sit-up or lift your leg toward your chest while standing. (Each of these muscles has other actions, too, but we’re focusing here on their hip-flexing ability.)

Of the whole group of muscles, the iliacus and psoas are the strongest hip flexors. They are often referred to as the iliopsoas because they both follow the same path and have exactly the same action. Although they have different origins, the psoas originates on the lumbar spine, while the iliacus originates on the inner surface of the ilium, one of the large bones of the pelvis—they come together in the very back of the lower abdomen and run across the floor of the pelvis, over the outer part of the pubic bones on each side, and dive deep through the inner upper thighs to attach to the inner upper femurs.

The iliopsoas is powerful partly due to its size—each psoas is nearly as big around as your wrist—and partly because its position so close to the hip joint gives it excellent leverage. It is such a strong muscle, in fact, that it can influence the tilt of your pelvis and therefore the position of your spine during your daily activities. In other words, when the iliopsoas gets very short and tight, it can pull your pelvis into an anterior (forward) tilt. This type of tilt—when the top rim of the pelvis tips forward and down and the coccyx (tailbone) lifts up—usually puts the lumbar spine into hyperextension, also known as a swayback. (A posterior or backward pelvic tilt happens when the top of the pelvis moves backward, the coccyx moves down and tucks under, and the lower back flattens.)

Strong abdominals help pull the pelvis into a posterior tilt, but you might be surprised to hear that even if you have strong abdominals, a tight and strong iliopsoas muscle can win the battle and tilt your pelvis forward.

A chronically hyperextended lumbar spine puts the lower back muscles in a shortened position, causing them to ache. If the only thing that relieves your lower back discomfort is to lie on your back and pull your knees to your chest (therefore flattening the curve), you might have a hyperextended lumbar. Hyperextension also puts a greater percentage of your upper body weight onto the small facet joints on the posterior aspect of the vertebrae instead of on the solid vertebral bodies and disks, where it’s meant to be.

Enter our hero Virabhadrasana I, which can stretch the hip flexors, correct an anteriorly tilted pelvis, and help decompress your lower back. But remember to focus on practicing it with a strong posterior pelvic tilt. If you don’t, and your psoas and iliacus are tight, they will pull the lumbar spine into hyperextension and you’ll eventually feel lower back compression and pain in the pose.

There are several ways to learn how to hold a posterior tilt, but it’s best to learn the action first before integrating it into the pose. Try this: Stand with your back to a wall, with your heels about a foot away from it and your knees slightly bent. Feel an anterior tilt by moving your frontal hip points (the two bony knobs on the front of your pelvis, each known as the ASIS, or the anterior superior iliac spine) down toward your thighs, your coccyx up the wall, and your lower back away from the wall. To move from an anterior to a posterior tilt, lift your ASIS (hip points) up away from your thighs, take your tailbone down the wall, and feel your lower back move onto or toward the wall.

Now that you’re familiar with the action of a posterior tilt, here’s how to incorporate it into your Virabhadrasana I.

Set up the pose with your back to a wall, right foot forward, left heel pressing into the wall. Place a strap over the upper thigh in the crease of your right hip and hold it down with one hand on each side of the thigh. Be sure to keep your chest lifted and your shoulders down. If you are in an anterior tilt, your hip points will drop down toward or even onto the belt. To counter that, bend both knees a little and strongly lift your hip points up off the strap and into a posterior tilt. With the hip points lifted, gradually straighten the left knee, pressing the heel into the floor but keeping the lift up on the hip points, in order to stretch the iliopsoas across the front of the left hip.

Keep in mind that for most students, when the front knee bends more deeply, up to the ultimate goal of 90 degrees, it’s hard to hold the posterior tilt. If that’s the case for you, try coming only partway into the pose.

You can deepen the iliopsoas stretch a bit more by minimizing the external rotation of the left hip. When you do this, your left knee will turn out less. The iliopsoas is an external rotator, so it will try to reduce the stretch by externally rotating as you do a posterior tilt. To lessen this, be sure to turn your left foot 6o degrees away from the wall. (In standing poses like Warrior II, you turn your foot out more, so that it’s parallel to the bottom edge of your mat.) Once you’ve done that, square your pelvis toward the wall across from you as much as you can by pressing the left inner heel down firmly, lifting the left inner knee, and using the strength of the left leg to help bring the left half of the pelvis forward. You’ll not only deepen the iliopsoas stretch, but you’ll also protect your left knee from a twisting injury.

As your iliopsoas stretches and lengthens, it will pull less on your lumbar spine and pelvis, and you’ll be able to lift your lower back up out of the pelvis, thereby decompressing it and relieving discomfort. You may also notice that your posture, both standing and walking, improves, and you might enjoy a new sense of space in your hips and lower back. And you can rest assured that you’re helping prevent arthritis in your lower back.

I hope I’ve made a convincing case for consistently including hip flexor stretches in your home practice. It’s especially important to work them on sedentary days and before practicing backbends—if you’re stuck in an anterior pelvic tilt, you can imagine the compression in the lower back while you do Setu Bandha Sarvangasana (Bridge Pose), Ustrasana (Camel Pose), or Urdhva Dhanurasana (Upward Bow Pose). When you consider adding Warrior I as your iliopsoas stretch, know that you’re setting the stage for a strong and healthy back in the years to come.

carsten höller brings people together through four giant slides in mexico city

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

descending from restaurants, offices, and a future hotel, the installation brings an unexpected element of play into the everyday rhythms of a mixed-use urban development.

Carsten Höller transforms movement through architecture into a collective experience with Mexico City Slides (2026), a permanent installation of four monumental spiral slides at Arte Abierto Polanco in Mexico City. Commissioned by Arte Abierto and developer SOMA in collaboration with architecture practice Sordo Madaleno, the work forms part of Antara’s latest expansion in Colonia Granada. Höller’s sculpture weaves through the mixed-use development, connecting restaurants, Google’s offices, the future Rosewood Mexico City hotel, and Arte Abierto’s new cultural venue through an unexpected invitation to slide.

Ranging from 12 to 38 meters in height and 31 to 90 meters in length, the four structures descend from different points across the complex before converging at a shared landing area. Each slide accommodates one person, allowing four visitors to descend simultaneously and arrive alongside one another. The installation is accessible to the public free of charge and is scheduled to open in December 2026.

Visitors arriving from restaurants, workplaces, and hospitality spaces enter the same physical experience, temporarily setting aside the social and functional distinctions attached to these environments. Höller describes this encounter as an equalizing gesture. Regardless of where people begin, they end up next to one another. Even for those who choose not to ride, the slides introduce an unusual spectacle into the development.

Höller has been working with slides as an artistic medium since 1998, exploring how an object usually associated with playgrounds can become part of an architectural setting. The Belgian artist describes them as ‘a sculpture with people inside’. The experience is as much about the physical sensations of sliding as it is about the structure itself.

Over the years, this idea has taken different forms. His projects include Test Site (2006) at Tate Modern in London, Victoria Slide (2011) at the New Museum in New York, Vitra Slide Tower (2014) at the Vitra Campus in Weil am Rhein, and Isometric Slides (2015) at the Hayward Gallery in London. He continued the series with Florence Experiment Slides (2018) at Palazzo Strozzi, DAC Slide (2020) at the Danish Architecture Centre, and another iteration of Isometric Slides (2021) at the Luma Foundation in Arles. More recently, Stockholm Slides (2025) became a permanent feature of Moderna Museet’s facade. In Mexico City, four slides bring this ongoing exploration into a larger architectural setting, connecting different parts of Antara through this shared experience.

For Höller, sliding is about more than the thrill of speed. The moment of descent brings together fear, excitement, and pleasure, as riders briefly give themselves over to gravity. He describes the sensation as a ‘voluptuous panic upon an otherwise lucid mind’, borrowing the phrase from sociologist Roger Caillois. There is a certain contradiction in the experience: the slide is a controlled structure, yet the feeling of losing control is what makes the ride exciting.

In Mexico City Slides, four people can set off from different parts of Antara and arrive at the same landing area, turning the ride into a shared experience. For a brief moment, people coming from a restaurant, an office, or a hotel find themselves side by side, brought together by something as simple as taking a slide.

The installation forms part of a wider transformation of Antara, which marks its twentieth anniversary in 2026. Originally developed by SOMA on a former industrial site in Colonia Granada, the destination opened in 2006 as Mexico City’s first open-air retail complex. Its latest expansion introduces additional retail and dining spaces, offices, a new public garden, and a cultural program led by Arte Abierto, SOMA’s nonprofit contemporary art foundation established in 2019.

Designed by Sordo Madaleno, the expanded development brings together landscaped pathways, stepped terraces, and a 2,500-square-meter public garden, connecting its different functions through new pedestrian spaces. The wider program also includes Now What (2026), an exhibition by Danish artist collective SUPERFLEX, which examines the relationship between human-made environments and natural forces.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/health-tech/hims-hers-inks-deal-eli-lilly-offer-branded-weight-loss-drugs" hreflang="en">Hims &amp; Hers inks deal with Eli Lilly to offer branded weight loss drugs</a>

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →
Hims & Hers inks deal with Eli Lilly to offer branded weight loss drugs" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Hims & Hers struck a deal with Eli Lilly to offer the drugmaker’s branded obesity treatments through its online health and wellness platform.

Under the agreement, Eli Lilly will give Hims & Hers pharmacies direct access to its U.S. Food and Drug Administration-approved GLP-1 medications at self-pay prices. Eligible patients on the Hims & Hers platform can be prescribed and order Eli Lilly's injectable Zepbound (tirzepatide) and daily oral medication Foundayo (orforglipron) without leaving the company's platform.

Prior to this agreement, Lilly drugs were available to patients on Hims & Hers only if they left the site and went to Lilly's direct-to-consumer platform, LillyDirect.

Shares of Hims & Hers jumped 8% Friday following news of the Eli Lilly partnership.

The agreement brings together Lilly’s obesity medicines and Hims & Hers’ consumer platform to expand consumers' access to FDA-approved weight loss drugs, the companies said.

"Patients can complete a consultation, receive a prescription if their provider determines it is appropriate and have an FDA-approved Lilly medicine shipped directly to their home, all in one seamless experience," the companies said in a press release.

Self-pay pricing starts at $149 per month for Foundayo and $299 per month for Zepbound.

"At Lilly, we are always looking for new ways to help more people get the medicines they need, and that means meeting patients where they already are," said Ilya Yuffa, executive vice president and president, Lilly USA and global customer capabilities, Eli Lilly and Company, in a statement. "Millions of people turn to Hims & Hers for their care, and through this agreement, those patients now have a simple, more direct way to get FDA-approved Lilly medicine at an affordable price if prescribed."

Hims & Hers' technology platform supports provider outreach, follow-up care and treatment planning. Weight loss patients on Hims & Hers receive a membership experience that includes 24/7 access to a care team, nutrition guidance, regular clinical check-ins and access to the Hims & Hers Weight Loss community, according to the company.

The agreement with Eli Lilly mirrors Hims & Hers' partnership with Novo Nordisk, which has been selling its branded weight loss drugs Wegovy and Ozempic through Hims & Hers since March. Novo had previously sued the telehealth company for offering compounded versions of its medications. Hims & Hers' agreement with Novo marked a shift in its obesity strategy toward FDA-approved branded GLP-1 treatments and away from cheaper compounded GLP-1 drugs.

Earlier this year, Hims & Hers took heat from the pharmaceutical manufacturer and federal regulators after announcing plans to launch a knockoff of Novo's oral pill. It backed down from those plans under pressure.

Hims & Hers stated that it never offered access to compounded copies of Lilly’s GLP-1 drugs and has committed not to do so in the future.

As part of the agreement, the companies have committed to protecting independent clinical decision-making, so that providers on the Hims & Hers platform continue to prescribe the treatment they determine is right for each patient.

“Lilly is one of the most innovative pharmaceutical companies in history, and I’m incredibly proud that Hims & Hers is a part of making sure more people can access the medicines they’ve created,” said Andrew Dudum, co-founder and CEO of Hims & Hers, in a statement. “By working together, we’re not just reaching more people with obesity with seamless, personal access to FDA-approved Lilly medicine. We’re showing the industry what innovators can achieve for customers everywhere when we work together to make the world well.”

Hims & Hers has faced financial pressure as it shifts from high-margin compounded GLP-1s to lower-margin branded weight-loss drugs. The company posted a $92 million loss in Q1, including $33 million in restructuring costs, largely from write-downs of its compounded GLP-1 supply chain, which executives said faced “risk of obsolescence.”

In Q2, Hims & Hers reported an $86 million loss, compared with a $43 million profit a year earlier. The quarter included $81 million in one-time costs, including expenses tied to its exit from compounded GLP-1s.

A night of telling performances: Fan player ratings from AC Milan 0-2 Ferencvaros

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

After another disappointing midweek performance, AC Milan fans have left their ratings to the team’s performance against Ferencvaros.

The derby win was a chance for Milan to build some momentum before the international break and they failed to take that chance against Ferencvaros. From the first minute, they simply weren’t good enough and the 2-0 defeat was hardly a shock.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Of course, this meant our own ratings were pretty low – as you’d expect – with Lorenzo Torriani given the worst rating. However, he wasn’t exactly an outlier. It was a night of poor performances, unacceptable from the Rossoneri.

Taking a look at the ratings that you, our readers, provided via our player ratings tool, the consensus was very similar. The highest rating was a 5.5 from the bench, with no starter scoring above 4.7. Like we say, telling.

Filippo Terracciano was the standout performer of the night, scoring a horrible 3.0 rating, whilst Goncalo Ramos and Ardon Jashari’s performances weren’t viewed well either, with both scoring a 3.8.

It’s a performance that leaves more questions than answers, especially with key players failing to make a difference, too. Koni De Winter scored a 4.0, Yunus Musah scored a 4.1 and Adrien Rabiot got a 4.6, so even the ‘starters’ weren’t on it last night.

In total 138 votes were submitted, a drop from the weekend’s win – but this just shows the effect of another lacklustre performance…

Audi May Invest an Additional $3 Billion in Georgia Factory: Report

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The German automaker just announced its $4 billion plans for its first U.S. plant, but it may already be considering a second phase of construction.

Audi hasn’t even broken ground on its newly announced $4 billion plans for a 1500-acre factory in Macon, Georgia, but the German automaker is already hinted at an additional $3 billion investment in the facility.

A new report from Reuters claims that Audi is already considering a second phase of construction at the Georgia plant. After pledging to invest an initial $4 billion, documents released by the Mid-Georgia Gateway Development Authority reportedly state that Audi will invest $3 billion more by 2033. Audi supposedly aims to employ an additional 3000 workers as part of the expansion plan.

The Trump Administration allegedly promised Audi some degree of tariff relief as part of the decision to build its first U.S. production facility; Reuters reported that Audi pushed for the European Union import duty to be lowered to 7.5 percent, but that the U.S. Commerce Department was expected to agree to around 10 percent. Currently, Audi imports all of its models to the U.S. from Europe and Mexico, and models from the EU are subject to a 15 percent tariff. Audi paid $1.4 billion in duties to the U.S. government last year.

Georgia is heavily incentivizing Audi’s decision to build in the Peach State: it is giving Audi the land, as well as providing $458 million in tax incentives and offering $120 million in grants. These grants will focus on infrastructure, rail improvements, environmental site remediation, and construction, according to Automotive News. The company will also be provided an additional $430 million in subsidies for water, road improvements, utility issues, workforce training, and recruitment.

Audi plans to produce its Q7, Q8, and Q9 SUVs at its future U.S. facilities. The German automaker isn’t limiting its U.S. production plans to Georgia, either: an additional 25,000 Audi units will reportedly be produced at an incoming Scout Motors factory in South Carolina. Audi pledged to build 200,000 vehicles annually at the Georgia plant by the early 2030s.

A New York transplant hailing from the Pacific Northwest, Emmet White has a passion for anything that goes: cars, bicycles, planes, and motorcycles. After learning to ride at 17, Emmet worked in the motorcycle industry before joining Autoweek in 2022 and Road & Track in 2024. The woes of alternate side parking have kept his fleet moderate, with a 2014 Volkswagen Jetta GLI and a BMW 318i E30 street parked in his Queens community.
