U.S. Treasury Cuts Off MEXC From U.S. Financial System Over Alleged $16 Billion in Illicit Flows

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The U.S. Treasury’s Financial Crimes Enforcement Network issued a final order Friday that will cut offshore exchange MEXC Global off from the American banking system over alleged links to sanctions evasion by Iran, North Korea and Russia, the department said.

FinCEN’s order, published as a PDF pending Federal Register placement, finds MEXC a “primary money laundering concern in connection with Russian illicit finance” under section 9714(a) of the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act. Thirty days after that Register notice, covered U.S. financial institutions will be barred from any funds transfer — including convertible virtual currency — to or from MEXC.

Reuters reported the same cut-off Friday afternoon. Treasury framed the move as part of Operation Economic Outcast. It is not an OFAC sanctions designation: the order says it does not rewrite Bank Secrecy Act duties or OFAC rules, and it is a FinCEN funds-transmittal ban rather than a Section 311 proposed rulemaking.

Treasury alleges MEXC facilitated at least $16 billion in illicit flows from May 2021 through May 2026, including nearly $1.5 billion tied to Russian sanctions evasion and about $22 million linked to Iran’s Central Bank and the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps’ Qods Force. The platform was still processing trades with sanctioned Garantex and successor Grinex as recently as June 2026, more than four years after OFAC designated Garantex, the release says.

The order, signed by FinCEN Deputy Director Jimmy L. Kirby, covers MEXC Global and named affiliates operating outside the United States, including Seychelles-incorporated MEXC Global Ltd / MX Global Ltd, Estonia’s MEXC Estonia OÜ, Switzerland’s GIDA Technology AG and the U.K.’s MXC Global Ltd, plus successors. Covered institutions face civil or criminal penalties for willful violations.

Until the Federal Register publishes the notice, the 30-day clock has not started. As of Friday evening eastern time the Register’s public index still showed no MEXC document, matching the order’s own cover note that publication is pending.

Founded in 2018 and formerly branded MXC, MEXC ranks among the world’s 20 largest centralized crypto venues by volume. Treasury puts daily turnover above $1 billion and reserves near $5 billion as of mid-2026, with access claimed across more than 170 countries. The exchange already lists the United States as a prohibited jurisdiction in its user terms, so Friday’s action mainly hits any remaining dollar rails, banking partners and counterparties that still touch U.S. institutions.

Regulators elsewhere have been circling for years. The U.K. Financial Conduct Authority put MEXC Global Ltd on its warning list in March 2024 as unauthorized. Hong Kong’s Securities and Futures Commission warned the same month that the platform was marketing to local investors without a licence. Seychelles’ Financial Services Authority said in May 2026 it took action against MX Global Ltd for unlicensed virtual-asset operations — footnoted in FinCEN’s order.

Those compliance gaps matter because the illicit-flow thesis is specific, not guilt by association. Beyond Russia, FinCEN cites about $1 billion in direct traffic with more than 40 Iranian crypto exchangers, nearly $26 million with the OFAC-sanctioned A7 sanctions-evasion network, and almost $200 million tied to the A7A5 stablecoin. DPRK facilitators and cybercrime flows are also alleged. MEXC has not issued a public response to the Friday order; its main X account kept posting marketing content through the afternoon.

Practically, once the order bites, U.S. banks, money transmitters and other covered firms cannot move dollars or crypto to MEXC wallets or accounts. Offshore users may keep trading peer-to-peer, but dollar on-ramps and any bank that still clears for MEXC become legal risk. The template tracks earlier 9714 cut-offs of venues such as Bitzlato, and it lands beside a busy sanctions week that has already seen North Korea crypto designations, an Iranian exchange action, and Russia’s push to put bitcoin trading on the Moscow Exchange.

Whether MEXC contests the finding, seeks an exemption, or quietly reroutes liquidity is the open question. For now the paper trail is clear: a final FinCEN order under 9714(a), a 30-day fuse after Federal Register publication, and Treasury’s claim of at least $16 billion in illicit volume sitting behind the cut-off.

This Coach’s Treadmill Strategy Maximizes Fitness, Minimizes Fatigue, and Busts Boredom. Here’s How to Execute It.

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

We earn a commission for products purchased through some links in this article. Why Trust Us?

As runners, sometimes we think throwing hills into our runs or pushing the pace a little extra in hard workouts can lead to more gains without consequence. In reality, every time you make your runs a bit faster, longer, or steeper, you have to ease up on something else or you risk burnout, injury, or simply not finishing the session.

Balancing each element of your workout takes some planning. But thankfully, David Siik, founder of Precision Run by Equinox and author of The Ultimate Treadmill Workout, has done the strategizing for you. Siik’s treadmill workout method packs in incline, speed, and distance, without leaving you overly fatigued or struggling to recover the next day. The extra payoff: zero boredom.

Siik’s method combines incline and speed, along with adequate recovery, to gain all the benefits of an interval workout that’s easy to follow. “Once you experience really well-balanced intervals like this, it becomes very easy to know when something feels risky or too stressful on the body,” Siik says.

When you change one element in this workout strategy, you automatically adjust the other elements to manage your fatigue levels and maintain efficiency. For example, when you run intervals at a 1 percent incline, you slow your speed down by 0.2 miles per hour (mph) to prevent overstressing your body.

“It prescribes the right speeds on the right inclines, for the right amount of time so that you are able to maximize your performance while minimizing impact on your body,” Siik says.

The pace for the run portions of this workout should be about 2 mph below your one-minute PR speed. For example, if your one-minute PR speed is 7 mph, run at 5 mph. This pace should feel like a 9 out of 10 for rate of perceived exertion (RPE), with 10 equaling all-out effort. It should feel like you could repeat those intervals a few more times without the incline at the end of your workout if you had to.

A shortcut to determining your one-minute PR is to first try running at 5 mph, then go off Siik’s easy guide:

Put that one-minute PR pace setting to use with this workout.

Your outdoor paces don’t always translate directly to the treadmill, Siik explains. You might pace yourself differently because running in place on the treadmill can slightly slow down your times, especially if you’re used to using a GPS watch outside or going off marathon pace, for example, Siik says. That’s why he uses his “one-minute PR” method to help you determine your treadmill pace.

If you go straight to a 9 out of 10 RPE in the very first interval of a 45-minute workout, you might hurt or overexert yourself, Siik explains. It’s okay to start off on the slower end of your effort for the first few intervals, especially as you get more comfortable adjusting to treadmill pace.

Siik recommends starting with a 6 out of 10, then building up from there. This helps you preserve energy for later intervals and leaves you more room to improve.

Even if you’re training for a race, you can still incorporate these workouts into your schedule, in lieu of your weekly interval workout. If you’re going long distance, like a marathon, Siik suggests lowering your run interval pace by 1 mph and bringing your walk breaks up to a pace that’s about 2 mph slower than your run pace.

Siik calls this “cushioning.” “You’re still doing interval training, but the contrast of the hard effort and the recovery become a little less intense,” he adds.

When you have a balance between the amount of speed, incline, and duration you’re running, you don’t overuse your muscles or find yourself struggling to recover. “Increasing workload (incline or speed) too quickly can lead to soreness or risk of injury,” Siik adds. “Small, calculated, progressive increases in incline and/or speed creates a smooth, even increase in work on the body.”

This treadmill workout strategy is designed to make sense to runners who have never run before; it teaches you how to switch back and forth between fast paces and recovery speed, which you might not be used to if you only ever log easy runs or speed up too much on rest intervals.

Most of the time, these types of workouts last between 30 and 45 minutes and include walk breaks, so you’re only running about 60 percent of the time, Siik notes. This makes the workout beginner-friendly.

Each workout follows a pattern, which keeps you more engaged in the intervals and anticipating what’s coming next. “Patternization is the best-kept secret in treadmill running. If you have a pattern that’s really well-structured, your mind knows where the workout is going, and it’s magic,” says Siik.

This also means you’re less distracted, making you more likely to stick with your workout paces and less likely to give up on the session. “When you have a workout that’s really structured and you’re paying attention to the intervals… You do better quality work, then it starts to make more sense to you, and you start making bigger and better goals,” Siik says.

Kristine Kearns, a writer and avid runner, joined Runner’s World and Bicycling in July 2024. She previously coached high school girls cross country and currently competes in seasonal races, with more than six years of distance training and an affinity for weightlifting. You can find her wearing purple, baking cupcakes, and visiting her local farmers market.

Canadian art market takes hit from trade war with US

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Tariff talk: Canadian prime minister Mark Carney with President Donald Trump Photo: public domain sourced/access rights from Sebastian Goll/Alamy Stock Photo

The fallout from the trade war between the Canadian prime minister Mark Carney and US president Donald Trump is having real-time consequences for many Canadian artists and galleries. Since 22 August the US has imposed tariffs of 50% on a wide range of items imported from Canada, including paintings, drawings, sculptures, prints, pastels, collages and more. Other tariff categories that include feathers, bones, tortoise shell, horns, antlers and other animal parts, will especially impact Indigenous artists. In early September Canada imposed counter-tariffs on hundreds of imports from the US, among them 50% on many materials used to make and transport art, plus certain printed materials including photographs; these did not include paintings, drawings or pastels imported from the US, which were already subject to 25% tariffs as of March 2025.

Mackenzie Sinclair, the executive director of the Art Dealers Association of Canada (ADAC) tells The Art Newspaper that the tariffs are “a significant concern for our members, given the close economic relationship between our two countries”. She adds that around two thirds of Canada’s cultural exports are destined for the US—in 2023, Canada exported C$27.1bn ($19bn) in cultural products, of which C$18.1bn ($12.7bn) went to the US.

“The art market as a whole is a fragile ecosystem, and changes to the conditions under which art is traded can have unforeseen and far-reaching consequences across the sector,” Sinclair says.

Sarah Macaulay, the founding director of Macaulay & Co Fine Art, a gallery with spaces in Vancouver and Toronto that represents artists like the First Nations painter Lawrence Paul Yuxweluptun. She tells The Art Newspaper that, until recently, around 10% of Yuxweluptun’s sales were to US buyers. “Now we’re more focused on Australia and Europe,” she says.

Macaulay says she has cancelled plans to participate in a fair during Miami Art Week this December. In the wake of the latest tariffs, she says, “it’s just too risky”.

Andy Sylvester, the owner and director of Vancouver’s Equinox Gallery, estimates that buyers in the US represented around 15% of his sales of his work until recently, but that will “likely be reduced to zero”. He adds: “US tariffs on Canadian made art will affect the gallery and our artists both financially and opportunistically.”

“Canadian art is not simply another category of merchandise,” Sinclair says. “It represents the work, livelihoods, cultural expression and international standing of Canadian artists and galleries. For a US collector faced with a substantially higher purchase price, the additional cost may lead them to postpone a purchase or acquire an artwork from a US-based artist or gallery instead. This creates a direct loss of competitiveness for Canadian artists and galleries.”

At Art Toronto, the country’s largest art fair, which is held every year in late October, US buyers represent a significant portion of annual sales.

“The ‘Buy Canadian’ rhetoric has been very prevalent, and it is heartening to see how communities across the country have been supporting artists now that these tariffs have been imposed,” says Emily-Jean Alexander, the fair’s director. “For this autumn’s Art Toronto we [built] new programmes to help those who want to support our national art market, but who are new to buying artwork to see the friendliness and accessibility that can be found at the fair and build confidence in supporting Canadian galleries and institutions.”

Insiders say import taxes are strangling much-needed cultural interchange

‘A rippling impact across our sector’: Canada’s art trade is seeking to lessen its dependence on US buyers to soften the blow of 25% tariffs on imports

After the US Supreme Court struck down the White House’s previous tariffs, Trump and his trade representative have imposed new tariffs of 10% or more on around 80 countries

With around 80% of its exhibitors based in Canada, this edition of the country’s biggest art fair reflects the current “elbows up” attitude of a nation embroiled in a trade war with its neighbour

Jürgen Klopp lauds El Mala brothers’ special Bundesliga moment in phone call with Said

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Germany national team head coach Jürgen Klopp has revealed his delight at the recent Bundesliga debut of 1. FC Cologne striker Malek El Mala. The Bundestrainer personally congratulated the player’s younger brother, Said, on the memorable occasion during a telephone conversation confirming the latter’s continued involvement with the senior national team.

Said El Mala is among the five recent international debutants retained in Klopp’s 24-man November squad for the upcoming Nations League fixtures against Serbia and the Netherlands. The 20-year-old has now revealed that his second telephone conversation with Klopp was considerably more relaxed than their first.

Speaking to Sky Germany, El Mala explained that the discussion quickly turned to his brother’s recent Bundesliga exploits.

“He calls you, tells you that you’re in the squad again, and then there are usually one or two other stories,” El Mala explained. “This time, he mentioned how happy he was that my brother came on and scored a goal, and how much he enjoyed seeing us celebrate together.”

Malek El Mala made his long-awaited Bundesliga debut during Köln’s 1-1 draw away against Union Berlin last Sunday. The 21-year-old came off the bench in the 72nd minute and scored the equalizer just seven minutes later.

As previously reported by Get German Football News, Said helped create his older brother’s debut goal, delivering the initial cross that Alessio Castro-Montes redirected toward Malek at the far post. The brothers subsequently celebrated together on the pitch, becoming the first siblings to feature together for Köln in a Bundesliga fixture since Klaus and Thomas Allofs during the 1986/87 season.

The occasion also proved deeply emotional for their family, who had travelled to Berlin to witness the historic moment.

“When we had a FaceTime call a day later, my mum had lost her voice,” Said revealed. “I think she even cried in the stadium. They were probably ten times happier in the stands than I was on the pitch.”

For Said, the special family occasion has now been followed by another opportunity to represent Germany.

Good Riddance: Tesla to End Cybertruck Production in 2027

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Tesla says it will wind down its most notorious model at Gigafactory Texas to make room for its self-driving Cybercab.

Tesla CEO Elon Musk announced the end of the brand’s most ostentatious model, the Cybertruck, in a post on X late Thursday night. Tesla will begin winding down production of the wedge-shaped pickup at its Texas Gigafactory “around the middle of next year,” Musk said, to boost manufacturing capacity for its Cybercab robotaxi.

Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production

The Cybertruck will not be missed by many. Sales have been so low that Tesla doesn’t even separate them out in its financials — lumping it into an “Other models” column — with experts estimating numbers significantly lower than Tesla’s built-out manufacturing capacity and far below Musk’s sales goals. Meanwhile, Tesla and Musk have increasingly shifted their focus to AI, robotics and autonomy as the brand’s future.

Tesla unveiled the edgy, all-electric pickup truck at an event in 2019, and the cracks were already beginning to show in many of Musk’s most outlandish claims surrounding the truck. (In the case of the “bulletproof” glass, the cracks literally showed.)

After multiple delays, deliveries finally began in 2023. Musk had spent the intervening four years baffling people by making (and subsequently retracting) many truly bizarre statements about the EV’s capabilities, but the first nail in the Cybertruck’s coffin was the starting price, which had ballooned to $60,990, up from the initial $40,000 target touted at debut.

Over its lifetime, the Cybertruck struggled to meet even the most basic expectations, contending with reliability issues, multiple recalls and a penchant for eating its owners’ fingers. It didn’t help that the sci-fi-styled EV arrived around the same time Musk became deeply involved in US politics and policy, and has become known as something of a rolling dog whistle for those who agree with the billionaire’s increasingly unpopular ideologies.

Customers were presented with a niche EV that was more expensive than expected, unsafe, unreliable, unpopular and unattractive. It’s no surprise that sales have suffered, with some experts predicting that since its arrival on US roads, Tesla has sold only about 10% of Musk’s Cybertruck goals. That’s despite Tesla building out an estimated 125,000-unit annual manufacturing capacity at the Texas Gigafactory where the truck is built, leaving the automaker with a huge stock of unsold, unwanted inventory sitting on its lots. One of Musk’s other companies, SpaceX, bought $131 million worth of Cybertrucks last year.

The Cybertruck’s 2027 wind-down will make way for increased capacity for the fully autonomous Cybercab, which debuted in 2024 and is slated to ramp up production in Texas next year. Pitched as a robotaxi that you could own for “under $30,000,” Tesla began offering rides in its steering-wheel-free Cybercab in limited parts of Austin in early September — a stunt that immediately prompted a federal investigation into whether the self-driving vehicle meets federal safety standards.

The Cybertruck joins the Model S and X on Tesla’s 2026 chopping block. The electric sedan and SUV were discontinued in January to make room for production of the Optimus humanoid robot at Tesla’s factory in Fremont, California. That will leave just the Model 3 and Model Y in Tesla’s consumer car lineup until the brand’s next rolling publicity stunt, the high-flying Tesla Roadster, allegedly arrives in 2028.

DNA Evidence Helps Convict Serial Poacher, Who Forfeited 2 Vehicles and Hunting Privileges for Life

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 6, 2026 4:25 PM EST

Get the hottest outdoor news from Outdoor Life.

A Wyoming man is serving 96 days in jail after he was convicted and sentenced on eight charges of wildlife violations this fall in Fremont County, according to a recent statement from the Wyoming Game and Fish Department.

Dakota Jordan of Lander, who was found guilty of poaching deer, elk, and moose since at least 2021, now owes the state $18,000 in restitution. He has also forfeited the animal parts, firearms, and two vehicles used in his poaching sprees — a truck and a Jeep. Finally, Jordan lost his hunting and trapping privileges for life, a penalty that applies in all 50 states.

“I do think the penalties are appropriate for his complete disregard for wildlife,” Jon Desonier, WGFD game warden and access coordinator tells Outdoor Life. “We take this stuff seriously. Guys aren’t just getting slapped on the wrist for serious wildlife crime.”

Desonier, who was named wildlife officer of the year in 2025, started investigating Jordan after a man came to his office with information about an illegally killed elk cow and calf in November 2024. Word of a poached moose and deer were already on Desonier’s radar, but the dots weren’t connecting. That changed during an aerial deer survey. A crew counting wildlife from the air spotted the headless carcass of an elk.

“We [had] spent a day walking around trying to find it and had no luck,” Desonier says. “We were not far, maybe 200 or 300 yards off, but they had a much better view from the air so it was easier to see.”

DNA from that kill site matched evidence found at Jordan’s property. Further DNA evidence proved damning: DNA from four other poached animals traced back to Jordan, thanks to a DNA database at the state-maintained forensics lab. Those hits included ivories from one elk, the leg bone of a second, meat from a third, and the European mount of a deer.

“I’m so thankful for our forensics lab in Laramie,” Desonier says. “They held on to DNA evidence and analyzed a ton of evidence for us. If it wasn’t for them, we would not have success in this case.”

Before the department could proceed with seizing the moose Jordan had poached, officials needed to determine if he’d killed it on state, federal, private, or reservation land. Using photos and GPS data from Jordan’s cellphone, investigators found the bones of a moose killed in 2022 and determined it had been poached on private land. The background of his photos included utility equipment, which was also key.

“We drove around looking for the transformer in the photo,” Desonier says. “A High Plains Power lineman saw the photo and sent us right to the kill site.”

Investigators suspect Jordan poached more animals than those in his conviction, but they focused on the cases with the strongest evidence

“In my opinion, this guy shouldn’t be hunting,” Desonier says. “He doesn’t have the mindset to respect wildlife. That’s how we got here.”

Read Next: Idaho Outfitter Gets Hunting Ban After Illegally Shooting a Trophy Bull in Utah

Jordan’s father also lost his hunting privileges as a result of the poaching investigation. He is banned from hunting for five years and also owes Wyoming $5,370. At least two additional suspects are currently under investigation in a multi-state operation.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/pharma/cms-makes-it-official-codifying-medicare-drug-price-negotiations-rolled-out-under-ira" hreflang="en">CMS makes it official, codifying Medicare drug price negotiations established under IRA</a>

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →
CMS makes it official, codifying Medicare drug price negotiations established under IRA" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Against a backdrop of repeated industry challenges in court, the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services (CMS) is locking in the drug price negotiation framework established under the Biden-era Inflation Reduction Act (IRA).

In turn, the process under which CMS selects certain high-spend Medicare Part B or Part D drugs and negotiates lower prices with manufacturers will carry on into 2029 and beyond. The rule codifies and expands policies previously established through guidance for the initial 2026-28 period.

The final rule is slated to go into effect Dec. 5, CMS said in a fact sheet accompanying Thursday’s announcement. By Feb. 1 of next year, CMS will select up to 20 drugs covered under Medicare Part B or Part D for price negotiations taking effect in 2029, with the potential to also select medicines included in previous rounds for renegotiation.

That upcoming group of drugs will make up the fourth cycle of pricing negotiations, with negotiated prices taking effect in 2029. CMS asserted that the rule gives both patients and drugmakers “a clear path forward as the agency builds on the program’s results and continues negotiating prices for high-cost drugs.”

The initial rounds of price negotiations have already included a slew of prominent brands, including Novo's semaglutide franchise of Wegovy and Ozempic and cancer meds like Bristol Myers Squibb’s Pomalyst, Pfizer’s Ibrance and AstraZeneca’s Calquence.

Meanwhile, the latest tranche of drugs selected earlier this year for negotiation, whose new prices would go into effect in 2028, includes notable entrants like Gilead’s HIV treatment Biktarvy, AbbVie’s Botox and Eli Lilly’s diabetes drug Trulicity, to name just a few.

Beyond codifying the framework for price negotiations in 2029 and subsequent years, CMS has also taken steps to fine-tune several elements of the program.

Namely, the rule finalizes a policy on how CMS classifies certain fixed-combination drugs, with the aim of addressing “potential program integrity concerns” that could encourage drugmakers to evade negotiated prices by steering patients toward new product formulations.

The rule also includes a provision establishing a temporary “small biotech floor” that prevents CMS from offering or accepting a counteroffer below a specified price floor for certain eligible small biotech drugs in 2029 and 2030. It also codifies two policies related to Medicare Part D intended to ensure plan coverage of medicines with a maximum fair price in effect and limit the prices plans pay pharmacies and other dispensing entities.

The IRA and the Medicare price negotiations it empowered have been frequent targets of the biopharma industry, which has broadly alleged that the policy is unconstitutional and that the pricing talks aren’t as voluntary as advertised. But attempts by major drugmakers and powerful trade organizations like the Pharmaceutical Research and Manufacturers of America (PhRMA) to push back on Medicare price negotiations have repeatedly stalled out.

In the interim, the U.S. government has made a separate and more recent push to rein in high prescription drug costs by leaning on President Donald Trump’s “most favored nation” (MFN) strategy.

“President Trump directed us to lower drug costs for Americans using every tool at our disposal,” CMS Administrator Mehmet Oz, M.D., said in a statement announcing the new rule. “This rule provides additional tools to help CMS negotiate better deals, achieve greater savings for taxpayers and beneficiaries, and improve health outcomes for seniors who rely on the Medicare Prescription Drug Program.”

MFN, for its part, has yielded at times, controversial deals with more than two dozen drugmakers, generally involving commitments to price new medicines in line with prices in high-income comparator countries and offer MFN pricing on certain existing drugs through Medicaid.

Through those deals, which have also included investment commitments in the U.S., drugmakers have been able to secure temporary exemptions from the Trump administration’s threatened pharmaceutical import tariffs.

Around this week’s midterm elections, several Big Pharma executives have signaled that they expect little immediate change to their thinking around MFN. Nevertheless, the lack of transparency surrounding the deals has already drawn scrutiny from Democratic lawmakers. With Democrats set to control both chambers of Congress beginning in January, the party may soon press harder to uncover the details of those agreements.

2025 summer signing is slowly becoming a key midfielder for Millwall under Alex Neil: Opinion

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Millwall have been a very dangerous side since Alex Neil was appointed boss. After narrowly missing out on promotion following a Championship play-off defeat to Hull City last term, the club are looking to bounce back once again.

Neil’s side have built a reputation for identifying players capable of making an impact in the Championship without spending excessively. This summer has seen Kyrell Lisbie come in and impress, while Lyndon Dykes has been an excellent free transfer.

While immediate impacts are always looked for with transfers, patience is also required sometimes. Derek Mazou-Sacko is becoming another example of that, and is showing that Millwall’s long-term recruitment working well despite a mediocre start to the campaign.

The French midfielder arrived from Rodez in August 2025 for a reported fee of around €500,000. There was little fanfare surrounding his arrival, but he has gradually established himself as an important part of Alex Neil’s side.

At just 22, he is already showing the qualities needed to become a reliable Championship performer. Mazou-Sacko is not a midfielder who will regularly grab headlines with goals, assists or eye-catching creative numbers.

His value lies in the less glamorous aspects of the game, and his latest statistics underline why Neil has increasingly trusted him. His aerial ability is another useful asset, standing at 6ft 1in, That physical presence helps Millwall compete for second balls and provides additional protection in front of their defence.

There is little suggestion that he should be expected to offer much more creatively. His five chances created place him in the 11th percentile, while his 440 touches rank in the 16th. However, that should not necessarily be viewed as a weakness.

He has completed 267 passes at an accuracy of 83.4%, including 46 line-breaking passes, showing he can retain possession and move the ball forward without taking unnecessary risks.

His development has been gradual. Having progressed through Troyes, spent time on loan at Nancy and subsequently made around 34 appearances for Rodez in Ligue 2, the move to English football represented an important opportunity.

He contributed to Millwall’s play-off push last season, making 23 league appearances and registering one goal and one assist. Now approaching 35 appearances for the club, he looks increasingly comfortable at this level.

Millwall will need more than one dependable defensive midfielder if they are to achieve their ambitions, but Mazou-Sacko is proving to be an excellent piece of business.

He might never become the most exciting player in Neil’s squad, but he makes the team more difficult to play through. Over a 46-game Championship season, that reliability can be just as valuable as an attacking match-winner.

Solana Targets Hong Kong's Tokenization Market

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Solana Company seeks to broaden its footprint in Hong Kong amid rising interest from major financial institutions in tokenized assets in the Asian financial hub.

As reported by the South China Morning Post on Nov. 6, the Nasdaq-listed firm is seeking to leverage rising demand for blockchain-based financial products as the Asia-Pacific region emerges as one of the major growth opportunities for its business.

Solana Company Chairman and CEO Joseph Chee thinks Asia-Pacific could be one of the most exciting markets for Solana's ecosystem.

The firm already has its Asia-Pacific headquarters in Hong Kong and is positioning itself as a provider of digital asset infrastructure and related services.

Last month, Solana Company was listed as one of Hong Kong's strategic enterprises by the Office for Attracting Strategic Enterprises. The designation emphasized the firm's potential role in Hong Kong's burgeoning digital asset sector.

The firm now aims to identify opportunities to offer traditional financial institutions blockchain infrastructure, especially for their work in tokenizing real-world assets.

Hong Kong has been pushing for tokenization as part of its efforts to become a global digital asset hub.

During Hong Kong FinTech Week earlier this week, Securities and Futures Commission CEO Julia Leung announced that regulators had already approved 17 tokenized investment products for retail investors.

In total, the products manage around $1.3 billion in assets (a 34% surge from last year).

Although the tokenization industry has been growing, Chee admitted that Hong Kong is not as advanced as the U.S. when it comes to tokenization.

Even so, the fact that Hong Kong's tokenization industry is still nascent might be an opportunity for firms that could offer the necessary infrastructure.

Solana Company is now looking into boosting its local presence in Hong Kong to leverage the rising demand.

Chee said the firm might be ready to scale up its local workforce from fewer than 10 employees to as many as 30.

The next six months will be crucial for assessing the growth of the local market.

Wynn is holding to its September 2027 date for the UAE’s first casino resort but it will now decide in February what form the opening takes. The U.S.-Iran war means its first customers may travel from down the road in Dubai and elsewhere in the UAE, rather than being the international guests it primarily wants.

Casino resort operator Wynn Resorts will likely decide in February precisely how to open its Ras Al Khaimah casino resort, CEO Craig Billings said on the company’s third-quarter earnings call on Thursday.

“We will make a decision about the format in which we open. And we’ll probably make that decision around February,” he said.

Wynn Resorts will decide around February how to open its $5.7 billion Wynn Al Marjan Island resort in Ras Al Khaimah, CEO Craig Billings said on the company's third-quarter earnings call. The UAE's first integrated gaming resort is still scheduled to open in September 2027, but Billings said the mix of source markets it draws "does impact how we open," a shift in tone from August, when he said Wynn generally does not do phased openings. He laid out three demand scenarios tied to the course of the U.S.-Iran war: if conflict continues, the resort leans on people already in the UAE; if it moderates, it adds the wider region; if it resolves, Wynn's original demand thesis applies. That original 2024 thesis skewed international, with 37% of gaming revenue projected from international high rollers and 29% from other international travelers. Billings now describes the UAE's wealthy, largely expat population as the base business that is "easiest to address," and named Western Europeans as the travelers most likely to be apprehensive about the region. Handover of finished zones to operations starts in early January and runs through July, with rooms going on sale "quite soon." Wynn has contributed more than $1.12 billion in cash to the joint venture, in which it holds a 40% stake, and the venture has drawn $1.76 billion on its construction loan.

USELESS Jumps 15% as Buyers Enter Sideways Memecoin Market

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Home » Altcoins » USELESS Jumps 15% as Buyers Enter Sideways Memecoin Market

A satirical meme coin stands out from the heavily-red market with double-digit percentage gains as some buying sentiment returns.

USELESS coin rose 15% after announcing its LONG launch, pulling buyers back into the token while conditions across the broader memecoin market stayed uneven. The move reversed near-term sentiment sharply, yet current signals still do not point to a clear or lasting recovery.

The LONG announcement gave USELESS a clear short-term catalyst and helped lift the price as traders stepped back in. A 15% gain stands out for a smaller, sentiment-driven token, where relatively modest shifts in demand can produce outsized swings, as seen after the midterms.

Useless coins being exposed for what they are. https://t.co/9TMXVejVNU pic.twitter.com/7fKu12menT

This price advance looks driven more by renewed attention around the launch itself than by any confirmed improvement in the wider trading environment. Event-driven moves can attract quick inflows, but they also tend to fade once the initial reaction cools or broader appetite softens.

The recovery faces at least two immediate risks: sellers using the bounce to exit positions, and market conditions that fail to support continued speculative demand. Without steady buying after the initial launch reaction, USELESS could struggle to hold its gains.

Memecoin price action depends heavily on momentum, attention, and liquidity. When any of those cools, a token that has risen quickly can also retrace sharply, especially if many buyers entered mainly for the nearest-term USELESS meme coin catalyst.

The 15% rise shows buyers remain willing to respond to new developments. Mixed market conditions, however, leave the meme rebound vulnerable until demand proves more durable.

For now, the LONG launch has brought fresh interest, but the token still needs consistent participation beyond the initial reaction if the move is to develop into something more lasting.

Physint star Bill Skarsgård has shared some more information about the game and suggested that it’ll have around eight hours of cutscenes like Hideo Kojima's other work.

Even before everything that’s happened with Physint over the past few months, it seemed like Hideo Kojima’s spiritual successor to Metal Gear Solid was a very long ways off. That feeling has only been cemented with the sudden turn over from PlayStation to Xbox, with Kojima even recently confirming that he hadn’t settled on a game engine.

That’s not even taking into account that Kojima Productions is also currently working on OD, which has only had a few trailers.

As likely as it is that Physint won’t be with us until the next console generation at the very earliest, there have been some signs that it’s moving along somewhat steadily. The lead actors for the game were recently confirmed after much speculation, and one of them, Bill Skarsgård, has seemingly given us an idea of how many cutscenes to expect.

Yangwang U9 Xtreme Breaks Its Own Top Speed Record With 312.7-MPH One-Way Run

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

It’s the first production vehicle to surpass 500 kilometers per hour.

China’s Yangwang U9 Xtreme hypercar already held the record for fastest production car, but it seems the company wasn’t satisfied with that. The U9 Xtreme just beat itself—and crossed the 500 kilometer-per-hour barrier doing it.

With German racing driver Marc Bessing behind the wheel (the same man who piloted the car for its previous records), the U9 Xtreme hit 503.2 km/h during a run in Abu Dhabi on November 1. That’s 312.7 miles per hour. Unlike the previous record of 496.2 km/h (308.3 mph) that was set at Germany’s Papenberg track, this time, Yangwang took its electric hypercar to a closed public road; about six miles of Sheikh Khalifa Bin Zayed International Road was closed for the record run.

“Unlike a dedicated test track, a public road presents a different set of challenges, making precision, stability and confidence in the vehicle more important than ever,” Besseng said. “Breaking a world record once is extraordinary but doing it again in such a different environment is truly special.”

The road itself required sweeping prior to each run, and Yangwang spent two days running on the road before setting the record on the final pass. The company says it inspected every meter of the path for potential debris, even picking up rogue wire particles that could’ve been deposited from the cleaning brushes sweeping up.

Both the FIA and Guinness World Records have recognized the record as the highest speed achieved by a production car in a single-direction run. Updates to the car are credited for breaking the 500 km/h barrier versus last year’s 496.2 km/h run: Yangwang says it adjusted the angle of the rear wing to increase rear downforce, tweaked the suspension software to increase rebound damping “in response to subtle undulations in parts of the road,” and, changed tire pressure to increase high-speed stability.

In case you were wondering, the U9 Xtreme is fitted with four electric motors producing more than 2958 horsepower. It uses a 1200-volt battery pack, which is an upgrade over the regular U9’s 800-volt pack. As for the tire, Yangwang says it uses a specially designed tire from Giti Tire. Yangwang itself is a subsidiary of BYD, China’s largest automaker.

The next closest car to the U9 Xtreme is the Bugatti Chiron Super Sport that hit 304.8 mph back in 2019. In other words, Yangwang didn’t need to set this latest record to take back the crown. It simply ran the car for the love of the game, and of course to break that 500 km/h barrier.

The video is a fascinating watch, as Bessing spends plenty of time warming the tires and brakes before going for the run. He keeps it at about half or a little over half throttle until he gets past 380 km/h. From there on out, it’s full throttle as the car flies up to 480 km/h and creeps the rest of the way up to—and beyond—500 km/h.

Lawsuit Against New York AG Letitia James over Letter Alleging Group's Event Was Connected to "White Nationalist Ideals" …

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Mostly law professors | Sometimes contrarian | Often libertarian | Always independent

can go forward on First Amendment coercion, First Amendment retaliation, and defamation grounds, a federal district court concludes.

From Magistrate Judge Kenneth Schroeder's Report and Recommendation Sept. 22 in Doyle v. James, affirmed Wednesday by Judge John Sinatra (W.D.N.Y.), plaintiff's allegations:

Plaintiff Paul Doyle ("Doyle") is the Senior Pastor at Cornerstone Church in Batavia, New York. Cornerstone is a small, rural church with a history of service and community outreach. Cornerstone has worked to build relationships with communities of color and was aggressive in their assistance to victims of the mass shooting in Buffalo.

Plaintiff Clay Clark ("Clark") is the owner and operator of The Thrive Time Show, a highly successful business podcast. Clark has twice been named the US Small Business Association Entrepreneur of the Year and is the recipient of other business awards.

"ReAwaken America" is a conservative political movement that hosts events throughout the country. The ReAwaken America Tour ("the Tour") was scheduled to host an event in Rochester, New York on August 12, 2022 at the Main Street Armory. However, after community members and local politicians spoke out against the event, the Armory cancelled it.

The Tour then reached out to Doyle to see if Cornerstone would be willing to host the event. Doyle met with groups from Rochester to try to understand their concerns and concluded that "the Tour was not what was being portrayed in the media and by government officials." Doyle thus agreed to host the event at Cornerstone "due to his strong belief that free speech should not be restricted and those who attempt to exercise it should not be intimidated, particularly by government officials." The event was scheduled for August 11-13, 2022.

On August 3, 2022, New York Attorney General Letitia James sent a letter addressed to Clark and General Michael Flynn—a co-organizer of the Tour—to Doyle's home address "c/o Cornerstone Church." Plaintiffs allege that the letter was "nothing less than an attempt to covertly intimidate and threaten the Plaintiffs into shutting down the event."

As New York's top law enforcement officer, I have significant concerns that the ReAwaken America Tour's upcoming event at the Cornerstone Church in Batavia, New York on August 12 and 13 could spur extremist or racially motivated violence. These concerns center around the event's proposed dates, which coincide with the five-year anniversary of the deadly Unite the Right rally in Charlottesville, Virginia, and past extremist statements made by yourselves and the other featured speakers on the tour.

I am especially concerned about featured speakers' regular allusions to white nationalist ideals connected to the "Great Replacement Theory," a conspiracy theory that warns of white genocide and efforts to replace native born Americans with immigrants. The theory is frequently linked to violent actions, including the racially motivated mass shooting that killed 10 people at a Tops Friendly Markets store in Buffalo. Especially in light of this racist mass shooting, and other recent episodes of racially motivated violence in New York and throughout the country, the Office of the Attorney General is concerned that such rhetoric could contribute to violent or unlawful conduct at the ReAwaken America Tour's upcoming event….

The Office of the Attorney General writes to remind you that New York law prohibits racially motivated violence, harassment, or interference with another person in the exercise of their civil rights. New York Civil Rights Law § 79-n empowers the Office of the Attorney General to investigate acts of violence, intimidation, threats, or harassment directed at people based on a belief or perception regarding an individual's race, color, national origin, ancestry, gender, religion, religious practice, age, disability, or sexual orientation. In addition to actual damages, any person who violates this statute can be held liable for $5,000 in penalties for each violation.

Additionally, New York Civil Rights Law § 40-c prohibits discriminating against another person in the exercise of their civil rights—including their right to peacefully protest—based on similar protected characteristics. Finally, New York Executive Law § 63(12) empowers the Office of the Attorney General to take action against any business engaged in significant fraud or illegality—including the violation of New York's civil rights laws.

The Office of the Attorney General has a duty to protect New Yorkers from extremist and racially motivated violence. We stand ready to investigate any violation of the laws above and, if necessary, to enforce them to the fullest extent available. You are therefore instructed to take all necessary steps to ensure that the event complies fully with the requirements of New York's civil rights laws and all other applicable state and federal statutes.

Your cooperation in ensuring a peaceful and law-abiding event will be greatly appreciated.

Plaintiffs allege that James's "inferences towards racial discrimination and fraud associated with the church and the event were publicized in numerous media outlets … which injured Pastor Doyle's reputation, that of his church, and the reputation of Mr. Clark as well." They further allege that James made no reasonable inquiry to verify her alleged concerns and that, had she done so, she would have learned "about the generous history of Cornerstone Church, the upstanding reputation of both Plaintiffs, and the fact that the Reawaken America Tour had not a single publicized act of violence at any of their events, nor has the tour been legitimately accused of any crimes, illicit behavior, or the violation of any laws or ordinances." Plaintiffs also allege that they had "reached out to law enforcement and local government officials to ensure the safety of the event and that all local rules, laws, and ordinances would be followed."

Plaintiffs further allege that no ReAwaken America event has ever been associated with the racial theories or violence with which James's letter purported to connect them, and her statements were "negligent, reckless and a dereliction of her duty to exercise due diligence in her representation as Attorney General and in doing so, is an abuse of power."

Plaintiffs allege that James's politically motivated letter created a "smear" on their names and damaged their community relationships. The publicity surrounding James's letter resulted in "numerous cancellations of ticket sales by patrons who originally planned to attend the event prior to hearing the derisive false reports."

Finally, plaintiffs allege that, as a result, they were forced to hire additional security to ensure the safety of the participants and attendees. Ultimately, the event "went off without a single issue." …

The court held that plaintiffs had adequately alleged the letter was unconstitutionally coercive:

To state a claim that the government violated the First Amendment through coercion, "a plaintiff must plausibly allege conduct that, viewed in context, could be reasonably understood to convey a threat of adverse government action in order to punish or suppress the plaintiff's speech." …

Here, after linking plaintiffs and the event's speakers to racist theories and past incidents of racist violence, defendant listed various state anti-discrimination laws and their penalties, concluding that her office "stand[s] ready to investigate any violation of the laws above and, if necessary, to enforce them to the fullest extent available." Plaintiffs allege that they perceived this letter as a thinly veiled threat meant to cause them to cancel the event, accompanied by the specter of prosecution.

As this Court stated in its prior RR&O [Report, Recommendations & Order]:

Here, defendant's letter has a heavy-handed, ominous tone and tells plaintiffs that: (1) she considers their own past statements concerning and "extremist," (2) she believes that "such rhetoric" could lead to "violent or unlawful conduct" at the upcoming event; and (3) she "stands ready" to investigate and enforce violations of New York laws, whose penalties she enumerates.

Such statements might reasonably be understood as threats as opposed to a government official's speech that is "nothing more than a well-reasoned and sincere entreaty in support of his own political perspective." …

[D]efendant's assertion that her letter can only be construed to convey a request to avoid or prevent unlawful conduct is unavailing at the pleading stage. In NRA v. Vullo (2024), the Supreme Court rejected such an argument:

For the same reasons, this Court cannot simply credit Vullo's assertion that "pursuing conceded violations of the law …is an obvious alternative explanation" for her actions that defeats the plausibility of any coercive threat raising First Amendment concerns… Of course, discovery in this case might show that the allegations of coercion are false, or that certain actions should be understood differently in light of newly disclosed evidence. At this stage, though, the Court must assume the well-pleaded factual allegations in the complaint are true.

Given this context, the fact that defendant's letter did not cause plaintiffs to cancel their event does not preclude a finding that defendant's letter was an unlawful attempt to coerce them to do so. See Okwedy v. Molinari (2d Cir. 2003) ("A public-official defendant who threatens to employ coercive state power to stifle protected speech violates a plaintiff's First Amendment rights.")…. Therefore, construing the allegations of the complaint in plaintiffs' favor, and considering the above authorities, the Court concludes that plaintiffs have plausibly alleged that they "reasonably understood" defendant's letter to be an attempt to suppress the exercise of their First Amendment rights. Bantam Books, Inc. v. Sullivan (1963).

The court also held that plaintiffs had adequately alleged the letter was unconstitutionally retaliatory:

"Where a private plaintiff sues public officials, the Second Circuit states the elements [of a retaliation claim] as follows: (1) Plaintiffs have a right protected by the First Amendment; (2) Defendants' actions were motivated or substantially caused by Plaintiffs' exercise of that right; and (3) Defendants' actions caused Plaintiffs some injury." … [D]efendant does not contest that plaintiffs satisfy the first element of a retaliation claim…. [And] plaintiffs' complaint alleges facts which, construed as true, would allow a reasonable person to infer that defendant was motivated to send her letter due to the political content of plaintiffs' speech ….

[D]efendant argues that plaintiffs fail to adequately plead that defendant's actions caused them legally cognizable harm. The Court discussed the issue of harm in its prior RR&O in the context of standing, noting that "[v]arious non-speech related harms are sufficient to give a plaintiff standing" for purposes of a First Amendment retaliation claim. In particular, the Court reviewed authority holding that "reputational harms" may constitute such an injury. The Court observed:

Here, plaintiffs' complaint is replete with allegations that defendant's letter falsely associated them with racist, "white nationalist ideals;" the "Great Replacement Theory;" a racially motivated shooting in Buffalo, New York; and other "racially motivated violence." Plaintiffs allege specific damages flowing from defendant's statements: injury to plaintiffs' reputations; harm to Doyle's community relationships, and specifically with communities of color; and an increased likelihood of violence towards plaintiffs….

Thus, construing the above allegations in plaintiffs' favor, the Court concludes that they have sufficiently alleged injury for purposes of their retaliation claim….

The magistrate judge also concluded that the law was sufficiently well-established that it recommended denying qualified immunity to the defendants. An excerpt:

[I]t has long been clearly established that "implied threats to employ coercive state power to stifle protected speech" violate the First Amendment. Hammerhead Enters., Inc. v. Brezenoff (2d Cir. 1983).

In 2003, the Court of Appeals reversed the dismissal of a complaint filed by plaintiffs against the Borough President of Staten Island who sent a letter to a billboard company with whom plaintiffs had contracted to produce and display billboards conveying plaintiffs' beliefs that, under biblical teachings, homosexuality is a sin. Okwedy v. Molinari (2d Cir. 2003). The letter stated that defendant and many community members found the billboards offensive, noting that the company "derives substantial economic benefits" from billboards on Staten Island and asking the company to contact defendant's legal counsel to discuss the matter. The billboard company took the signs down the same day.

The Court of Appeals held that plaintiffs had adequately pled a First Amendment claim; that a jury could find that the defendant's letter contained "an implicit threat of retaliation;" and that a "public-official defendant who threatens to employ coercive state power to stifle protected speech violates a plaintiff's First Amendment rights." …

And the court held that plaintiffs had adequately alleged defamation, despite defendant's claim of absolute privilege:

"… [A]n executive official is absolutely privileged to publish false and defamatory matter of another in the exercise of his executive function if the matter has some relation to the executive proceeding in which the official is acting." … In determining whether absolute privilege applies, the court considers two factors: (1) the personal position or status of the speaker, and (2) the subject matter of the statement and the forum in which it is made in light of the speaker's public duties. Under the second prong, it is not sufficient that the subject matter of defendant's speech merely "relate to" his public duties. Rather, the speech must be made "during his performance of an essential part of his public duties."

Under this authority, defendant is not entitled to dismissal of plaintiffs' defamation claims based on absolute immunity. The general duties of the New York Attorney General are set forth in New York Executive Law § 63. As relevant here, they include: the prosecution and defense of all proceedings in which the state is interested; the investigation, upon the request of the governor or other state officials, of certain indictable offenses; upon the request of the commissioner of labor or the state division of human rights, the prosecution of civil actions for discrimination; the prosecution of criminal offenses; and application to state courts for orders enjoining fraudulent or illegal acts.

In her current papers, defendant also cites N.Y. Exec. Law § 79-n(3), which provides that the attorney general may apply in the name of the state to a court to enjoin unlawful bias-related violence of intimidation. She also cites a penalty provision of an equal rights law that states that notice of any action under that section must be served on the attorney general.

However, the letter to plaintiffs was not written to fulfill any of these duties because defendant had initiated no investigation, prosecution, or any other governmental process against plaintiffs. In fact, defendant does not argue that plaintiffs had engaged in any actual or suspected acts that would fall within the scope of her law-enforcement authority…. Absent some factual predicate for a government official to believe that a citizen may have engaged in conduct that warrants the scrutiny of his or her office, that official is performing no duties that the doctrine of absolute privilege is meant to protect….

Tricia Sophia Lindsay represents plaintiff.

NEXT: Florida Ban on Material Support to "Domestic Terrorist Organizations" Can't Be Applied to Legal Advice to CAIR

Eugene Volokh is the Thomas M. Siebel Senior Fellow at the Hoover Institution at Stanford, and the Gary T. Schwartz Distinguished Professor of Law Emeritus and Distinguished Research Professor at UCLA School of Law. Naturally, his posts here (like the opinions of the other bloggers) are his own, and not endorsed by any institution. He is also the co-host of the Free Speech Unmuted podcast.

Apple’s grey list: Words and phrases your iPhone won’t help you spell

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

There are more than 100,000 words and phrases that the tech giant has restricted its predictive text from completing, research shows

“Australia is a very bad place to question your sexuality on MDMA with NSA spies” might be a text you would wish to send to your friends, if you have had an extraordinarily complicated night out in Sydney.

But your iPhone won’t help you spell any of it if you try to swipe-type it – that is, by gliding a finger across a keyboard as opposed to pecking out a word.

New research shows that Apple has a “grey list” of more than 100,000 words and phrases that it has restricted from being completed by its predictive text.

So if a user wishes to type phrases such as “Dictator Trump”, “NSA spies” or, surprisingly, “Australia is a very bad place”, “question your sexuality” or “on MDMA”, they will have to spell them out.

Apple’s predictive text will not suggest these words. And, if a user attempts to swipe-type them, they will often stumble, changing “United Ireland” to “United Iceland”, for example, or “Tibetan independence” to “Tibetan independences”.

The “grey list” was discovered by the independent researcher Derik Kauffman, whose other projects include using AI to find errors in scientific papers. After noticing that he had problems typing certain phrases – but not others – on his iPhone, Kauffman was able to unearth a table containing the list, encoded in Apple’s operating system.

The list he found includes some unsurprising words and phrases. Apple’s iPhone will not autocomplete references to drugs, sexual acts and, perhaps in the spirit of corporate protection, “iMessage isn’t working”.

But it also includes possibly politically charged phrases, which Kauffman described to Reuters as a “light-touch form of censorship”. Among these are “Trayvon Martin”, the name of the Black teenager shot in Florida in 2012, “Kashmir dispute”, “Muslim extremist” and “male chauvinist pig”.

In Hebrew, Apple will not autocomplete the phrase “the occupied territories”. In Arabic, it will not autocomplete the phrase “the Hamas movement”.

The list is one more glimpse into the dark bowels of how autocorrect – the chirpy keyboard partner that helps you look more educated to your loved ones – actually works.

The technology has taken many forms. In its earliest version, Unix, an operating system, would look up each word a user typed against a table of words, to determine if this word was spelled correctly.

This evolved into n-grams, a tool which allows an operating system to statistically predict what words might come next, based on the words before it. These form the basis of Apple’s familiar autocorrect, which was known to change expletives into prudish alternatives: “fork” and “ducking”, among other gaffes.

Last year, Apple launched a new autocorrect – a “transformer-based” system that was more akin to an LLM. Reddit sleuths saw this as a possible reason behind a spate of avant-garde spelling suggestions their iPhones made last year.

This hard-coded list illuminates one longstanding mystery behind the world’s spelling coach. Others remain, such as a brief glitch in which users of the dictation feature saw the name “Trump” flash across their screen when they spoke the word “racist” into their iPhones – which Apple quickly remedied.

Apple said that it designed its keyboard to avoid offering inappropriate or offensive predictive suggestions. It said iPhone users could always type out specific words or phrases, and override predictive text suggestions to achieve their desired meaning.

Amazon’s AI shopping agent moves into text messages, taking orders and tracking prices

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

by Todd Bishop on Nov 6, 2026 at 8:07 amNovember 6, 2026 at 9:35 am

Amazon is rolling out a new feature to let customers shop by text message — sending requests to its Alexa for Shopping AI agent, which will search for products, compare options and place orders on their behalf.

The idea is to make shopping work more like messaging a personal assistant. Amazon says the agent works in the background after it gets a request or a photo, and texts back when the task is done.

It’s part throwback and part hot new thing, reflecting a broader shift in how tech companies are offering AI agents for everyday use. OpenAI added texting to Dot, its personal AI agent, last month, and DoorDash rolled out text ordering in September.

The expansion of Amazon’s AI shopping assistant comes amid a larger fight over who controls online shopping when agents do the buying. Amazon has moved to block outside agents from Meta, Perplexity, Google and OpenAI from shopping its site while building its own.

The text thread is also a new place for Amazon to sell. The company says order updates, delivery notifications and deals will eventually land in the same conversation.

Digiday reports that sponsored products can appear among the options the agent texts back, free to advertisers for now, while purchases the agent completes on its own stay ad-free. Amazon reported $68.6 billion in advertising sales in 2025.

The text feature started rolling out Friday to a small group of U.S. customers by invitation, and millions more will get access in the coming weeks, the company says.

Customers who opt in get a contact called “Alexa Shopping” and can send it requests that range from a note that they’re out of dog food to a photo of a recipe they want turned into a grocery order, according to Amazon’s examples.

The agent searches, texts back with options and completes the purchase with the customer’s approval. Customers can also turn on auto-buy to have it purchase an item automatically when the price hits their target. It can also watch for price drops, reorder past purchases and check on packages, and Amazon says a conversation started by text will carry over to the Amazon app, the website and Alexa devices.

Amazon launched Alexa for Shopping in May, merging its Rufus shopping chatbot with Alexa+, the generative AI version of its voice assistant.

Let there be LEDs: Tech upgrade helps James Turrell’s Skyspace shine again at UW art museum

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Geek Life: Fun stories, memes, humor and other random items at the intersection of tech, science, business and culture.  SEE MORE

by Kurt Schlosser on Nov 6, 2026 at 8:01 amNovember 6, 2026 at 9:42 am

For most of the past dozen years, a James Turrell artwork on the University of Washington campus in Seattle — built to glow at sunrise and sunset — has been running on daylight alone.

That changes Nov. 14, when UW’s Henry Art Gallery relights “Light Reign,” its Turrell Skyspace, after a restoration that replaced aging lights with hundreds of new fixtures and a control system synced to the sun.

Originally installed in 2003 near the Henry’s entrance along 15th Avenue N.E., “Light Reign” is one of dozens of skyspaces by Turrell, the 83-year-old artist known for turning light itself into his medium.

Inside the artwork’s oval chamber, LEDs bathe the walls in slowly shifting color, and when the weather cooperates, a retractable lid opens to an oculus that frames the sky. From outside, the glass-paneled shell lights up with its own program — a 21st-century mood ring just steps from a bronze George Washington who has been gazing west toward the Olympics since 1909.

The relighting comes as the Henry, Washington’s first public art museum, heads into its 100th year. The museum opened on campus in February 1927, and officials are hinting that bigger changes are on the way for the centennial.

Trouble with “Light Reign” started around 2015, said Sage Sommer, the Henry’s director of exhibitions and collections. The original lights, by then more than a decade old, were reaching the end of their lifespan, and over time the museum lost the ability to run them.

Through those years, the Skyspace stayed open. Visitors could still sit inside, meditate and watch clouds and birds pass over the oculus, just without the color.

After years of planning, the Henry settled on a full lighting overhaul that also folds in ideas Turrell has used in his newer skyspaces. The exterior already ran on LEDs, but a very early generation of them, while the interior and oculus were lit by neon tubes made by Western Neon.

The museum didn’t work with Turrell directly. Instead it worked with lighting designer Ryan Pike, whose company, RHMP, works only on Turrell installations, from upgrades of existing pieces to new projects. Pike has worked on Turrell artworks since 2013.

For Pike, the toughest part was the exterior. “It’s just over 700 lights, in nine DMX universes, so each fixture can output independently of the rest,” he said in an email. DMX is the standard protocol for controlling stage and architectural lighting. Splitting the facade across multiple universes lets every fixture be programmed on its own.

Sommer said the Skyspace isn’t strictly time-based media, a museum term for art that relies on equipment and unfolds over time. But it raises the same questions. Sometimes the fix is simple, like converting a VHS tape to a digital file. Other times the hardware is integral to the work. Some artists insist a piece be shown only on a CRT TV, and some come back with a remastered version and want only that one shown.

“We work really closely with the artists to make sure that it is as they envision,” she said.

The Henry isn’t the only museum to face the problem. In 2023, Minneapolis’ Walker Art Center restored its own Turrell skyspace, “Sky Pesher,” after it became impossible to find the cold cathode tubes — a 1920s-era technology — that powered its sunrise and sunset lighting program.

Working with Turrell and his studio, the Walker switched to LEDs, which allowed more control and a much wider range of color. What had been “shades of tinted white” is now multicolored, Joe King, the Walker’s director of collections and exhibition management, said in an interview published by the museum.

The Henry’s new system uses roughly 840 LED fixtures, about 710 outside and 130 inside, Sommer said. The exterior program kicks in a half-hour before dawn and dusk. The morning show is brief, about 36 minutes, while the evening one currently runs about four hours after sunset.

“The very cool thing about this space, because we are so far north, is just how connected to sunrise and sunset it is, and how drastically different it is throughout the year,” Sommer said.

The roof has its own engineering pedigree. The Skyspace’s hydraulic lid, which lifts and slides out of the way, was designed by submarine manufacturers in Everett. It stays shut in the rain.

The museum closed the Skyspace in late July and used the downtime for physical restoration, too: repainting the interior, refinishing the teak benches, waxing floors, replacing worn locks, refitting doors that had swollen and cleaning out the gutters. Everything had to be rewired, and the project took longer than expected.

“Light Reign” is the most visible piece in the Henry’s collection of about 30,000 objects, including more than 18,000 textiles and 7,000 works on paper. Only a sliver is ever on view, so the Henry is putting its collection database online. The project was funded by UW’s Student Technology Fee. Anyone can book an appointment at its study center to see a piece up close.

Technology is also the focus of the Henry’s centennial exhibition, opening in late February. Swagato Chakravorty, associate curator, said he was surprised that no major museum had mounted a large, ambitious show on contemporary art and technology in decades, even as Seattle was transformed around it. His show looks at technology “from below,” focusing on the people and communities most directly affected rather than a “great man” story.

The exhibition is loosely organized around three threads. One is bioethics, including a work the Henry commissioned in 2002 with UW geneticist Mary-Claire King, which is being revived 25 years later. Another pairs Indigenous knowledge with tools like 3D printing and algorithms. The third is technology’s material toll, from data centers to water use.

Chakravorty said he deliberately avoids taking a side on whether technology is good or bad for art.

“Writing is a technology. Cave painting was a technology,” he said. As for AI: “It’s not going to kill art.”

The public unveiling of the refurbished “Light Reign” runs 3:30 to 6 p.m. Nov. 14, with remarks from Henry Executive Director Kris Lewis and the exterior illumination starting at 4 p.m. Admission is free, though the Henry encourages RSVPs, and interior viewing is limited to 25 people at a time for about 10 minutes each.

Bitcoin ETFs could surpass mutual funds in AUM by 2028: Bloomberg analyst

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

As we are about to enter the third year of the Spot Bitcoin [BTC] ETF being launched, it appears that BTC ETFs have a significantly stronger influence on BTC price returns than corporate Bitcoin accumulation.

According to the latest analysis by Ecoinometrics, an inflow of 10,000 BTC into spot Bitcoin ETFs corresponds to an estimated 3.2% boost in monthly Bitcoin returns. In comparison, the same amount of Bitcoin purchased by public companies contributes approximately 0.7% to monthly returns.

This suggests that ETF purchases have roughly 4.5 times the impact of corporate buying on Bitcoin’s price performance.

Adding more to this analysis, Bloomberg’s James Seyffart suggests that ETFs could surpass mutual funds in total Assets Under Management (AUM) within the next two to three years.

Their growth is only accelerating. If we stay on the same trend we’ve been on since the summer it will happen in ~two years.

Confirming his prediction, Syeffart used a chart and highlighted that ETFs accounted for 40.21% of total fund assets as of September 2026. And given the recent growth trends, ETFs could reach the 50% mark as early as September 2028 if the three-month growth rate continues.

However, other projections estimate this milestone between November 2028 and December 2029, depending on the period considered.

At the same time, ETFs rolling 12-month growth rate have also reached 3.74% in September 2026, above the historical average of 2.78% since the 2020 COVID market bottom. This indicates that ETF adoption is expanding faster than its long-term average.

This comes as BTC ETFs are in their outflows streak. According to Farisde Investors data, from 1st to 5th November 2026, Bitcoin ETFs saw mostly outflows. In fact, so far in November, it has already recorded $869.82 million in outflows. The only exception being on the 4th of November, when the BTC ETFs recorded a net inflow of $74.6 million.

All of this happened as the price of BTC was trading at $76,102.46 after a drop of 52% in the past 24 hours.

That said, besides Bitcoin ETFs, Ethereum [ETH] ETFs also recorded $272.09  in outflows, and Binance [BNB] ETFs recorded $271.01K in outflows. Meanwhile, XRP ETFs were funded by $529.51K in outflows, Solana [SOL] ETFs had $1.18M in outflows, and Hyperliquid [HYPE] ETFs also saw $7.66 million in outflows.

In short, the pattern suggests risk-off sentiment across crypto at the start of November, and not just in Bitcoin. So, given the current data, it remains to be seen whether Seyffart’s predictions turn into reality or not.

Sullivan Sidelined As West Ham’s Parker Decision Revealed

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Claret & Hugh can reveal today that, despite reports to the contrary, David Sullivan had no input into the decision to appoint Scott Parker as West Ham United’s new manager.

Some reports in the national media have suggested that Sullivan reacted to the Hammers’ back-to-back defeats against Southampton and West Bromwich Albion by convincing the board to sack Nuno Espírito Santo and appoint Parker. However, information received by C&H indicates that this was not the case.

Instead, we understand that the decision was driven by West Ham chief executive Karim Virani, who, following meetings on Monday, approached the board for permission to dismiss Nuno and appoint Parker. The former Hammers midfielder was already the preferred candidate, with one club insider describing him to us today as “the perfect fit”.

And it has very much been Virani liaising with Daniel Kretinsky which has prompted the club to change direction so quickly.

Karim Virani was the driving force behind the appointment of Scott Parker

The concern surrounding Nuno’s position had been building for some time and was not simply a reaction to two disappointing results. There was growing dissatisfaction within the club over performances across a number of matches, with the defeat to West Bromwich Albion effectively proving to be the last straw.

Virani subsequently decided to act more quickly than originally anticipated, with performances showing no sign of improvement. As Claret & Hugh revealed earlier this week, Monday’s meeting brought forward a dismissal which had initially been expected to come after further consideration.

Parker was very much at the top of the list, and Virani sought the board’s approval to proceed with the change. Our latest information makes an important distinction between the board approving the appointment and Sullivan personally driving the decision.

Far from being a Sullivan-led intervention following the Southampton and West Brom defeats, C&H understands that it was Virani who took the initiative to end Nuno’s tenure and bring Parker back to West Ham.
